## Production Paradigm & Cross-Section Decision Trees

All specifications in this manual govern a tool-neutral, declarative, event-driven production architecture observed by planner/author users and artist/director users:

| Production Concept         | Canonical Neutral Specification                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| :------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Geometric Primitives**   | `planar element` (`billboard` / `card`), triangulated or quad `mesh`, parametric `curve` (`spline`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **2D Coordinate Space**    | Special case of 3D space where elements reside on the `+X/+Y` plane (`Z = 0`) and the camera looks down the `−Z` axis (orthographic or narrow-FOV perspective).                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **Temporal Architecture**  | Discrete timed events parameterized by `(start, duration, intensity, channel, easing)` following the note-event paradigm of the [MIDI 1.0 Detailed Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fmidi.org%2Fmidi-1-0-detailed-specification).                                                                                                            |
| **Authoring Verbs**        | `event`, `cue`, `trigger`, `channel`, `sequence` — replacing manual per-frame curve handle dragging with declarative event blocks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Scene Representation**   | Static, declarative, exchangeable scene description + immutable asset workspace, conceptually aligned with [OpenUSD](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fopenusd.org%2Frelease%2Fintro.html) and [Khronos glTF 2.0](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FKhronosGroup%2FglTF%2Fblob%2Fmain%2Fspecification%2F2.0%2FSpecification.adoc). |
| **Effects Pipeline**       | Composable, deterministic, shader-based post-processing DAG executed in scene-linear color space.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Rendering Architecture** | GPU rasterization pipeline ([Khronos Vulkan Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fdocs.vulkan.org%2Fspec%2Flatest%2Findex.html)); zero runtime ray tracing or path tracing.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Precomputation (Bake)**  | Offline precomputation of heavy lighting, physics simulations, and cloth/fluid dynamics into static textures, lightmaps, spherical harmonics probes, or geometry caches.                                                                                                                                                                                                |

### Master Decision Tree 1: Selecting a Palette & Harmony Architecture

1. **Is the primary communication goal high-urgency warning, binary conflict, or maximum visual salience on a crowded feed?**
   - **Yes** → Select **Complementary Harmony** (`Δh = 180° ± 10°` in [OKLCH](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F)) at a strict **85:15 area ratio** (dominant neutral/desaturated field vs. high-chroma accent) to prevent simultaneous-contrast edge vibration ([Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color)).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 2.
2. **Does the scene require calm cohesion, luxury editorial restraint, or data hierarchy without hue distraction?**
   - **Yes** → Select **Monochromatic** (`Δh = 0°`, varying Lightness `L` across `0.15–0.92` and Chroma `C` across `0.02–0.18`) or **Analogous Harmony** (`3 hues` separated by `Δh = 25°–40°`), enforcing a **60:30:10 area distribution** ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 3.
3. **Does the scene need high-energy contrast while avoiding the harshness of a direct 180° complement?**
   - **Yes** → Select **Split-Complementary Harmony** (base hue `h₀` + two hues at `h₀ + 150°` and `h₀ + 210°` in OKLCH) with area weights **65% base : 25% secondary : 10% accent**.
   - **No** → For multi-character or playful categorical differentiation, use **Triadic Harmony** (`Δh = 120°`, keeping 2 hues desaturated `C ≤ 0.08` and 1 hue saturated `C ≥ 0.16`) or **Tetradic/Rectangular Harmony** (`Δh = 60°, 180°, 240°`, capping maximum simultaneous high-chroma hues at 2 per frame).

### Master Decision Tree 2: Selecting an Easing Family for an Event

1. **Is the property continuous and constant-velocity (e.g., clock sweep, seamless background pan, UV scroll, turntable rotation)?**
   - **Yes** → Use **Linear** (`cubic-bezier(0.0, 0.0, 1.0, 1.0)`).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 2.
2. **Is the element entering the frame from off-screen or materializing from an impulse/impact?**
   - **Yes** → Use **Ease-Out (Deceleration)**:
     - Subtle UI/typography settle: **Cubic Out** (`cubic-bezier(0.22, 1.0, 0.36, 1.0)`, `duration = 250–400 ms`).
     - High-energy kinetic snap: **Exponential Out** (`cubic-bezier(0.16, 1.0, 0.3, 1.0)`, `duration = 350–600 ms`).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 3.
3. **Is the element exiting the frame permanently or accelerating into a cut?**
   - **Yes** → Use **Ease-In (Acceleration)**: **Quadratic In** (`cubic-bezier(0.55, 0.085, 0.68, 0.53)`) or **Cubic In** (`cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.0, 0.67, 0.0)`, `duration = 150–300 ms`).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 4.
4. **Is the element repositioning from Point A to Point B while remaining visible on screen?**
   - **Yes** → Does it require organic physical mass and overshoot?
     - **No (Clean editorial/corporate/Swiss style)** → Use **Asymmetric Cubic In-Out** (`cubic-bezier(0.65, 0.0, 0.35, 1.0)` or faster acceleration/longer deceleration `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0.0, 0.2, 1.0)`, `duration = 350–700 ms`).
     - **Yes (Tactile/characterful/playful)** → Use **Damped Spring** (`ζ = 0.65–0.82`, natural frequency `ωₙ = 12–22 rad/s` for 1–2 subtle overshoots) or **Back In-Out** (`overshoot s = 1.2–1.7`, peaking at `4–8%` beyond target). Reserve **Bounce** (`restitution e = 0.45–0.65`) strictly for rigid gravity impacts, and **Elastic** (`ζ = 0.25–0.45`) strictly for stylized cartoon rubber/string mechanics ([Williams, The Animator's Survival Kit](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FThe_Animator%2527s_Survival_Kit)).

### Master Decision Tree 3: Selecting an Editorial Cut or Transition

1. **Are the two shots part of the same continuous scene, time, and location?**
   - **Yes** → Is the subject performing a physical movement across the shot boundary?
     - **Yes** → Execute a **Cut on Action** (`2–3 frames` before the peak velocity or impact in Shot A to the continuation in Shot B, with camera angle change `≥ 30°`) ([Murch, In the Blink of an Eye](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FIn_the_Blink_of_an_Eye_%28Murch_book%29); [Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F)).
     - **No** → Is dialogue or voiceover driving the transition?
       - **Yes** → Use an **L-Cut** (audio trails `12–36 frames` into the reaction shot) or **J-Cut** (incoming speaker's audio leads `6–18 frames` before the video cut).
       - **No** → Use a standard **Hard Cut** (0-frame transition).
   - **No (Changing time, space, or conceptual state)** → Proceed to Step 2.
2. **Is the change of state abrupt and intended to shock, awaken, or contrast emotionally?**
   - **Yes** → Use a **Smash Cut** (`0 frames`, pairing a high-contrast `≥ 12 LU` audio transient drop or spike with an immediate visual luminance/scale inversion).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 3.
3. **Is there a strong geometric form, motion vector, or silhouette shared between Outgoing Shot A and Incoming Shot B?**
   - **Yes** → Use a **Graphic Match Cut** (0-frame cut or `4–8 frame` cross-dissolve where focal object screen coordinates match within `≤ 3%` of frame width and velocity vectors align within `±15°`).
   - **No** → Proceed to Step 4.
4. **Does the transition represent a soft passage of time (`hours` to `years`) or contemplative emotional blending?**
   - **Yes** → Use a **Dissolve** (`24–72 frames` at `24 fps`, interpolated in linear light to prevent mid-point darkening).
   - **No (Motion-graphics sequence transitions: Wipe, Push, Whip, Zoom, Luma, Mask Reveal)** → Use **Mask Reveal / Wipe** (`12–24 frames`) when foreground geometry naturally traverses the frame, or **Whip/Slide/Push** (`6–14 frames` with directional motion blur `180°–360°` shutter equivalent) only when motivated by existing camera momentum. Never chain more than 2 stylized motion transitions consecutively `[OPINION]`.

## 1. Pre-Production

### 1.1 Brief, Objective, and Audience Specification

Definition: A structured, machine-readable contract defining the single communication objective, target viewer constraints, platform geometry, and measurable success thresholds before any scene authoring begins.
Why:

- Emotion: Aligns every downstream visual and auditory cue toward a single target affective state rather than contradictory tonal signals.
- Attention: Constrains information density to match the viewing environment's typical gaze commitment (e.g., feed scroll vs. theater seating).
- Production value: Eliminates mid-production style drift and incoherent asset assembly that expose amateur planning.
- Clarity: Enforces a single primary message proposition per deliverable so the user's audience retains the core takeaway.
- Cost: Prevents expensive late-stage re-bakes of 3D lighting, simulations, and voiceover recordings caused by undefined deliverables.
  Rules:

1. State the core message in `≤ 15 words` containing exactly one primary verb and one target outcome `[OPINION]`.
2. Specify delivery aspect ratio (`16:9`, `9:16`, `1:1`, `4:5`, or `2.39:1`), master resolution (`1920×1080` or `3840×2160`), and exact frame rate (`23.976`, `24`, `25`, `29.97`, `30`, or `60 fps`) per [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709) and [ITU-R BT.2020](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2020-2-201510-i%2Fen) before laying out any planar or 3D element.
3. Define the primary viewing distance and screen diagonal: mobile hand-held (`30–40 cm`, `15 cm` diagonal, viewing angle `~20°`), desktop monitor (`50–70 cm`, `60 cm` diagonal, viewing angle `~45°`), or living-room/cinema (`2.0–3.5×` picture height per [ITU-R BT.2100](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2100)).
4. Lock the target emotional valence (`[-1.0, +1.0]`) and arousal (`[0.0, 1.0]`) coordinates for each structural act of the sequence `[OPINION]`.
   Event mapping: Scene root metadata block declaring `duration_total`, `fps`, `aspect_ratio`, `color_space_working`, `color_space_output`, `audio_target_lufs`, and global sequence markers (`act_1_start`, `climax_cue`, `cta_cue`).
   Raster/bake note: N/A — Pre-production specification operates at the scene metadata and pipeline configuration layer prior to GPU rasterization.
   Cultural notes: East Asian commercial briefs frequently prioritize high-context relational mood and brand harmony before product utility, whereas North American and Northern European direct-response briefs prioritize explicit problem-solution hooks within the first `3.0 seconds` `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Dual competing objectives in one brief → split visual hierarchy where neither message registers; unspecified viewing device → typography and fine shader detail become illegible when a desktop-authored `16:9` master is viewed on a mobile screen.
   Checklist: [ ] Single `≤ 15-word` objective defined [ ] Aspect ratio, resolution, and frame rate locked [ ] Target viewing environment and sound-on/sound-off ratio specified [ ] Target valence/arousal trajectory declared
   Sources: [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709); [ITU-R BT.2020](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2020-2-201510-i%2Fen); [ITU-R BT.2100](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2100); [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152).

### 1.2 Script, Copy, and Reading-Rate Budgeting

Definition: The time-coded verbal and typographic text of a production, budgeted in words per minute (WPM) and characters per second (CPS) against visual action windows.
Why:

- Emotion: Controls vocal breathing room and dramatic pauses that allow emotional beats to land.
- Attention: Prevents verbal-visual interference (dual-coding overload) where simultaneous dense speech and dense on-screen text cause viewer drop-off.
- Production value: Well-paced copy sounds deliberate and authoritative rather than rushed or time-compressed.
- Clarity: Ensures spoken voiceover and on-screen kinetic/caption text remain within human phonological processing limits.
- Cost: Locking word counts before animation prevents re-timing entire event sequences when voiceover recordings overrun the timeline.
  Rules:

1. Budget spoken voiceover at `130–150 WPM` (`2.1–2.5 words/second`) for conversational/educational clarity, `110–125 WPM` for dramatic/cinematic gravitas, and never exceed `170 WPM` (`2.8 words/second`) even in high-energy promotional spots `[UNVERIFIED]`.
2. Budget on-screen display copy (non-caption titles and callouts) at `≤ 12–15 CPS` (`≤ 180 WPM`) and `≤ 7–10 words` per visual card so the viewer can read the text and still inspect the visual subject ([Netflix Timed Text Style Guide / Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external)).
3. Allocate mandatory silent breathing holds of `12–24 frames` (`0.5–1.0 s`) between major topical sentences to allow cognitive consolidation ([Chion, Audio-Vision](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FAudio_vision.html%3Fid%3Dis_HyxAyMkMC)).
4. Never display on-screen text that differs semantically from simultaneous spoken voiceover; either match key anchor words verbatim or place text during vocal pauses `[OPINION]`.
   Event mapping: Sequence of `audio_vo_cue` events (`start`, `duration`, `transcript`, `phoneme_markers`) paired with `text_display_event` cues whose `duration ≥ max(1.5s, char_count / 14.0)`.
   Raster/bake note: N/A — Script and copy budgeting occurs at the temporal sequencing layer before geometry or shader evaluation.
   Cultural notes: Fast-cut social feeds (TikTok, Reels, Shorts) tolerate `155–165 WPM` with zero inter-sentence pause, whereas broadcast documentary and European cinema standards favor `115–130 WPM` with `1.0–2.0 s` atmospheric pauses `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Writing `200+ WPM` copy for a fixed `30-second` slot → voiceover sounds breathless and pitch-shifted, leaving zero frames for visual reaction; mismatching spoken words and simultaneous screen text → viewer reads neither and loses comprehension.
   Checklist: [ ] Voiceover word count within `110–150 WPM` budget [ ] On-screen display text `≤ 15 CPS` [ ] Minimum `0.5 s` pause between major clauses [ ] Zero semantic contradiction between simultaneous VO and screen text
   Sources: [Chion, Audio-Vision](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FAudio_vision.html%3Fid%3Dis_HyxAyMkMC); [Netflix Partner Help Center](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external).

### 1.3 Moodboard, Style Frames, and Visual Anchors

Definition: Curated visual reference matrices (moodboards) and full-resolution rendered key frames (style frames) that lock the exact lighting, color palette, typography, texture, and post-processing look of each sequence prior to animation.
Why:

- Emotion: Tests and verifies the exact affective tone of color, light, and texture combinations on a static frame before committing to motion.
- Attention: Validates focal hierarchy and luminance contrast ratios at the exact delivery aspect ratio.
- Production value: Establishes a bespoke art-directed signature (e.g., Swiss typographic precision vs. tactile stop-motion clay) rather than generic default shaders.
- Clarity: Gives planner/author users and artist/director users an unambiguous visual target for every scene.
- Cost: Catching shader, palette, or typographic flaws on 4 static frames costs `< 5%` of fixing them across `1,800` animated frames.
  Rules:

1. Produce at least `1` Hero Style Frame per distinct narrative scene (or `1` every `10–15 seconds` of runtime) rendered at `100%` final delivery resolution through the exact target raster shader and color transform pipeline ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
2. Verify every Style Frame in three diagnostic views: (a) Full color, (b) Grayscale luminance only (`Y = 0.2126R + 0.7152G + 0.0722B` per [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709)) to confirm focal hierarchy survives without hue, and (c) Downscaled thumbnail at `10%` resolution (`192×108 px` or `108×192 px`) to confirm silhouette readability ([Birn, Digital Lighting and Rendering](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2F3drender.com%2Flight%2Findex.html)).
3. Extract explicit numeric tokens from approved Style Frames: exact OKLCH palette swatches, key-to-fill light intensity ratios, camera focal length (`mm`), depth-of-field aperture (`f-stop`), and post-process bloom/grain intensities.
   Event mapping: Static snapshot of the declarative scene graph evaluated at a single hero timestamp `t_hero` with all lighting, material, and post-processing channels active at reference intensity.
   Raster/bake note: Style frames must be rendered using the exact target GPU raster pipeline and baked lightmap/probe architecture rather than an offline path tracer, so material response and shadow softness match final production capability.
   Cultural notes: Bauhaus and Swiss International Style frames rely on asymmetric grid tension, flat high-contrast planes, and objective grotesque typography ([Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F)); Memphis and Neo-Brutalist frames employ clashing high-chroma primaries, thick black outlines (`3–6 px`), and geometric pattern fills.
   Failure modes: Creating moodboards only from third-party path-traced films without testing in the target raster pipeline → final output falls short of approved look; skipping grayscale thumbnail check → hero subject blends into mid-tone background (`ΔL < 0.15` in OKLab).
   Checklist: [ ] `≥ 1` style frame per scene at native delivery resolution [ ] Passes BT.709 grayscale luminance hierarchy check [ ] Passes `10%` thumbnail silhouette check [ ] Numeric palette, lens, and shader tokens extracted
   Sources: [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709); [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Birn, Digital Lighting and Rendering](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2F3drender.com%2Flight%2Findex.html); [Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F).

### 1.4 Storyboard, Animatic, Shot List, Asset List, and Schedule

Definition: The sequential blueprint translating script and style frames into timed shot panels (storyboard), a frame-accurate temp-audio video edit (animatic), and a dependency-ordered inventory of all required 2D/3D assets, bakes, and events.
Why:

- Emotion: Validates macro-pacing, comedic or dramatic timing, and audio-visual sync before spending effort on fine splining or simulation bakes.
- Attention: Reveals dead zones where visual state remains static for too long (`> 4.0–5.0 s` without motivated internal motion or cut).
- Production value: Ensures seamless screen-direction continuity and intentional camera progression across cuts.
- Clarity: Verifies that every narrative beat is visually legible in sequence at real-time playback speed.
- Cost: Prevents building or baking high-resolution assets for shots that get trimmed or cut during editorial pacing review.
  Rules:

1. Draw or block a new storyboard panel for every cut AND for every major intra-shot camera move or compositional shift (minimum `2–3 panels` per moving shot: start frame, midpoint, end frame) ([Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F)).
2. Assemble the **Animatic** (or 3D layout **Previs**) locked to the scratch/final voiceover and temp music beat grid at the exact master frame rate, establishing every shot's exact `frame_in`, `frame_out`, and `duration_frames` ([Williams, The Animator's Survival Kit](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FThe_Animator%2527s_Survival_Kit)).
3. Add a `12–24 frame` (`0.5–1.0 s`) head and tail handle budget to every baked 3D simulation or camera cache in the Shot List to allow editorial trim adjustments without re-baking `[OPINION]`.
4. Classify every item in the Asset List by screen-coverage tier: **Hero** (`> 25%` screen height; high-poly mesh `20k–100k tris`, `2048×2048` or `4096×4096` textures), **Secondary** (`8–25%` screen height; `2k–15k tris`, `1024×1024` textures), or **Background/Prop** (`< 8%` screen height; `< 2k tris` or planar billboard card, `256×256`–`512×512` textures) `[OPINION]`.
   Event mapping: Top-level sequence manifest (`shots[]`) where each shot entry defines `shot_id`, `start_frame`, `duration_frames`, `camera_id`, `active_asset_ids[]`, and coarse blocking events (`transform_event` with stepped/hold interpolation for animatic phase, upgraded to eased curves in production).
   Raster/bake note: Use low-sample viewport rasterization (flat/matcap shading + screen-space ambient occlusion) to render 3D Previs animatics at `< 16 ms/frame` before committing to lightmap or simulation bakes.
   Cultural notes: Japanese television anime production boards (_ekonte_) explicitly annotate frame counts down to the exact exposure sheet (_timesheets_ on 1s, 2s, or 3s) alongside camera pan speed in millimeters per frame, whereas Western commercial boards emphasize key emotional style frames `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Skipping the timed animatic and animating directly from static boards → shots run `30–50%` too long or too short for the voiceover; building `4K`-textured meshes for background props occupying `4%` of frame height → wasted VRAM and texture mipmap aliasing.
   Checklist: [ ] Every shot has start/mid/end storyboard panels [ ] Frame-accurate animatic locked to audio track [ ] Asset list tiered into Hero / Secondary / Background budgets [ ] `12–24 frame` head/tail handles included on all baked shot ranges
   Sources: [Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F); [Williams, The Animator's Survival Kit](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FThe_Animator%2527s_Survival_Kit); [OpenUSD Documentation](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fopenusd.org%2Frelease%2Fintro.html).

## 2. 2D Composition

### 2.1 Grids, Alignment Systems, and Visual Hierarchy

Definition: The division of the `+X/+Y` image plane into modular spatial columns, rows, margins, and gutters that govern element placement and establish a strict 3-level reading order.
Why:

- Emotion: Conveys structural order, institutional authority, and calm confidence; deliberate grid-breaking injects localized rebellion or surprise.
- Attention: Reduces saccadic search time by aligning edge transients along shared vertical and horizontal axes.
- Production value: Shared alignment axes and proportional gutters are the primary visual divider between professional Swiss-grade design and amateur layout ([Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F)).
- Clarity: Visually groups related information via spatial proximity and separates distinct data tiers.
- Cost: Parametric grid snapping eliminates manual per-element coordinate guesswork across aspect-ratio adaptations.
  Rules:

1. Construct a modular grid of `12 columns × 8 rows` for `16:9` frames (or `6 columns × 12 rows` for `9:16` vertical frames) with outer margins set to `5%–8%` of the shortest frame dimension and inter-column gutters set to `1.5%–2.5%` of frame width ([Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F)).
2. Enforce a maximum of `3` distinct visual hierarchy levels on screen simultaneously (Primary Focal Anchor, Secondary Supporting Context, Tertiary Metadata/Caption), separated by a minimum scale ratio of `1.414:1` ($\sqrt{2}$) or `1.618:1` ($\phi$) between levels ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
3. Maintain a macro-to-micro whitespace ratio of `≥ 2.5:1`: the distance between separate content groups must be at least `2.5×` the internal padding or line spacing within a group ([Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F)).
4. Restrict simultaneous text alignment axes to at most `2` per frame (e.g., all elements left-aligned to Column 2 and Column 7); never mix centered, left-justified, and right-justified text blocks within the same visual band.
   Event mapping: Declarative layout coordinates expressed in normalized grid-cell units `(col_start, col_span, row_start, row_span)` resolved to world `+X/+Y` planar coordinates at scene compilation.
   Raster/bake note: N/A — 2D grid coordinates resolve deterministically in vertex transformation prior to rasterization.
   Cultural notes: The Swiss International Style (Josef Müller-Brockmann, Karl Gerstner) mandates strict mathematical grid adherence and flush-left ragged-right sans-serif type; East Asian editorial layouts (Japanese _tategaki_ / modular magazines) frequently combine dense multi-axis cellular grids with vertical and horizontal typographic flow ([Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F)).
   Failure modes: Near-miss alignment (elements offset by `2–8 px` from a shared axis without clearly breaking the grid) → reads as an accidental layout error; equal spacing between all elements → flat hierarchy where the viewer does not know where to look first.
   Checklist: [ ] Modular column/row grid defined with `5%–8%` outer margins [ ] Maximum 3 hierarchy levels with `≥ 1.414:1` scale separation [ ] Macro-to-micro whitespace ratio `≥ 2.5:1` [ ] Zero near-miss (`2–8 px`) alignment offsets
   Sources: [Müller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fgrid-systems-in-graphic-design%2F393087%2F); [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F).

### 2.2 Framing, Rule of Thirds, Golden Ratio, Negative Space, and Balance

Definition: The proportional distribution of positive visual mass and open negative space across the frame to control equilibrium, tension, and focal gaze weight.
Why:

- Emotion: Symmetrical central framing evokes monumental stability, authority, or clinical detachment; asymmetric off-center framing with expansive negative space evokes dynamism, isolation, or contemplation ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
- Attention: Places primary subjects at high-salience intersection nodes (`1/3` or `1/φ`) where natural eye-scanning patterns converge.
- Production value: Intentional negative space (`≥ 35%` of frame area) prevents the cluttered, "fill-every-pixel" look common in amateur motion graphics.
- Clarity: Separates the figure from the ground so silhouettes and motion trajectories read instantaneously.
- Cost: High negative-space compositions require fewer background assets and reduce fragment shader overdraw.
  Rules:

1. Place primary focal anchors within `±3%` of either the **Rule of Thirds** intersections (`X ∈ {0.333, 0.667}`, `Y ∈ {0.333, 0.667}`) or the **Golden Ratio (Phi Grid)** intersections (`X ∈ {0.382, 0.618}`, `Y ∈ {0.382, 0.618}`), reserving exact dead-center (`0.500, 0.500`) strictly for formal symmetry, tunnel/portal shots, or rapid short-form caption lockups ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F)).
2. Preserve a minimum of `35%–50%` of total frame area as **Negative Space** (uncluttered background with local luminance variance `σ_L < 0.05` in OKLab) in standard narrative/editorial frames, and `≥ 65%` in luxury/minimalist brand frames `[OPINION]`.
3. Balance asymmetric compositions using the visual fulcrum law: a small, high-contrast/high-chroma/moving element at distance $d_1$ from frame center balances a larger, low-contrast/static element at distance $d_2$ when $\text{Area}_1 \times \text{Contrast}_1 \times d_1 \approx \text{Area}_2 \times \text{Contrast}_2 \times d_2$ ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
4. Provide **Lead Room (Nose/Motion Room)**: allocate `60%–65%` of horizontal free space _in front_ of a moving or looking subject's vector, unless intentionally cramping the subject against the frame edge (`< 15%` lead room) to signify psychological trapping or imminent collision ([Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F)).
   Event mapping: Planar element or camera framing offset event setting normalized screen-space anchor `(u, v)` to `(0.382, 0.618)` or `(0.333, 0.667)` with a lead-room offset vector proportional to subject velocity `v_subject`.
   Raster/bake note: N/A — Framing and spatial balance are geometric layout properties evaluated prior to pixel shading.
   Cultural notes: Classical Chinese and Japanese ink-wash aesthetics (_ma_ / emptiness) treat negative space as an active, substantive medium rather than an absence; Western maximalist retail/arcade graphics frequently compress negative space below `20%` to simulate sensory abundance `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Placing a profile or moving subject looking/moving toward the nearest frame edge without narrative justification ("short-siding") → creates accidental claustrophobia; tangential contact where a subject's edge barely kisses the frame border or another object's contour → flattens depth and creates visual irritation.
   Checklist: [ ] Focal anchor at `1/3`, `1/φ`, or deliberate `0.5` symmetry [ ] Negative space occupies `≥ 35%` of frame area [ ] Asymmetric visual weight balanced across center fulcrum [ ] `60%–65%` lead room in direction of gaze/motion [ ] Zero accidental tangents with frame edges
   Sources: [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Mascelli, The Five C's of Cinematography](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F).

### 2.3 Safe Areas, Storyboarding Continuity, Cuts, Edits, and 2D Effects

Definition: The protective boundary margins inside the active raster and the cross-shot screen-location continuity rules that prevent essential graphic elements from being clipped by displays or lost by the viewer's eye across 2D cuts.
Why:

- Emotion: Preserves compositional poise across cuts so the viewer stays immersed in the narrative flow.
- Attention: Minimizes the eye's re-acquisition distance (`Δpos` across a cut) so gaze locks onto the incoming focal point within `100–150 ms` ([Murch, In the Blink of an Eye](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FIn_the_Blink_of_an_Eye_%28Murch_book%29)).
- Production value: Prevents logos, lower thirds, and kinetic titles from colliding with television bezels or social platform UI overlays.
- Clarity: Guarantees 100% visibility of critical text and icons across broadcast and mobile viewports.
- Cost: Authoring within universal safe zones allows a single master render to pass multi-platform QC without re-layout.
  Rules:

1. For `16:9` broadcast/streaming deliverables, keep all critical action inside the **Action Safe Area** (`93%` width × `93%` height, i.e., `3.5%` margin on all four edges) and all legible text/logos inside the **Title Safe Area** (`90%` width × `90%` height, i.e., `5.0%` margin on all four edges) per [SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den) (or the legacy EBU/broadcast `80%` width × `90%` height title safe when targeting older CRT/overscan distribution).
2. For `9:16` vertical social deliverables (`1080×1920`), enforce asymmetric **Platform UI Safe Zones**: keep top `12%` (`230 px`), bottom `22%–25%` (`420–480 px`), and right `15%` (`160 px`) clear of essential text or faces to prevent occlusion by status bars, captions/descriptions, and engagement buttons ([TikTok In-Feed Ad Specs](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fads.tiktok.com%2Fresources%2Fhelp%2Farticle%2Ftiktok-auction-in-feed-ads%3Flang%3Den); [Meta/Instagram Reels Specs](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.facebook.com%2Fbusiness%2Fhelp%2F1038071743007909)).
3. Maintain **Gaze Anchor Continuity** across fast 2D cuts (`shot duration < 48 frames` at `24 fps`): place the incoming shot's primary focal point within a radius of `≤ 15%` of frame width from the outgoing shot's final focal point ([Murch, In the Blink of an Eye](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FIn_the_Blink_of_an_Eye_%28Murch_book%29)).
4. Limit 2D drop shadows and layer glows on graphic elements to motivated depth separation: drop shadow opacity `12%–28%`, blur radius `1.5%–4.0%` of element height, and vertical offset `0.5%–1.5%` of element height `[OPINION]`.
   Event mapping: Scene-level bounding constraint validator clipping or flagging any `text_element` or `hero_planar_element` whose world-to-NDC bounding box exceeds `[x_min_safe, x_max_safe] × [y_min_safe, y_max_safe]`.
   Raster/bake note: 2D layer effects (drop shadow, inner glow, stroke bevel) should be evaluated as separable 2-pass Gaussian/distance-field fragment shaders on the element's planar quad rather than multi-sampling geometry in 3D space.
   Cultural notes: East Asian variety television and live-stream graphics intentionally populate the outer perimeter with persistent multi-colored _telop_ reaction banners while keeping the center `60%` clear for action; Western cinema and prestige streaming strictly forbid persistent perimeter banners `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Placing subtitles or CTA buttons in the bottom `15%` of a `9:16` vertical video → completely covered by platform username and caption UI; jumping the focal point from far top-left to far bottom-right across a `15-frame` cut → viewer misses the incoming subject entirely.
   Checklist: [ ] `16:9` graphics inside `90%` SMPTE ST 2046-1 Title Safe [ ] `9:16` graphics clear of top `12%`, bottom `25%`, and right `15%` UI zones [ ] Gaze anchor jumps `≤ 15%` frame width on fast cuts (`< 2 s`) [ ] 2D drop shadows stay within `12%–28%` opacity
   Sources: [SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den); [Murch, In the Blink of an Eye](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FIn_the_Blink_of_an_Eye_%28Murch_book%29); [TikTok In-Feed Ad Specs](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fads.tiktok.com%2Fresources%2Fhelp%2Farticle%2Ftiktok-auction-in-feed-ads%3Flang%3Den); [Meta/Instagram Reels Specs](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.facebook.com%2Fbusiness%2Fhelp%2F1038071743007909).

## 3. Typography

### 3.1 Classification, Pairing, and Licensing Verification

Definition: The structural selection and contrast-pairing of typefaces across Vox-ATypI classifications (Humanist, Transitional, Modern/Didone, Slab Serif, Grotesque/Neo-Grotesque, Geometric, Display, Monospace) with verified broadcast/embedding rights.
Why:

- Emotion: Typeface morphology carries immediate affective tone before a single word is decoded (e.g., Geometric/Grotesque = objective modernity; Didone = high-fashion elegance; Humanist = warmth; Monospace = technical precision) ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
- Attention: High structural contrast between headline and body classifications creates instant visual differentiation between information tiers.
- Production value: Pairing two nearly identical typefaces from the same classification (e.g., two Neo-Grotesques) reads as an accidental font substitution mistake.
- Clarity: High x-height (`≥ 0.52` of cap height) and open apertures preserve character differentiation under motion blur and video compression.
- Cost: Selecting variable fonts or a single superfamily with 4–6 weights reduces asset memory footprint and licensing overhead.
  Rules:

1. Limit any production to a maximum of `2` typeface families (or `3` only when a dedicated `Monospace` family is required for tabular numerals/timecode) ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
2. Enforce the **Concordance-or-Contrast Rule**: either use a single superfamily across varying weights/widths (e.g., Light vs. Bold Condensed) OR pair two classifications with unmistakable structural contrast (e.g., High-Contrast Display Serif headline + Neo-Grotesque Sans body); never pair two distinct families from the same sub-classification ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
3. Avoid extreme hairline strokes (`stroke width < 1.5 px` at delivery resolution), such as unmodified Didone hairlines on body text, because 4:2:0 chroma subsampling and temporal inter-frame compression cause thin serifs to shimmer and vanish `[OPINION]`.
4. Use `tabular-nums` (`tnum` OpenType feature) for all animated counters, HUD readouts, timers, and financial figures so character widths remain constant (`Δwidth = 0 px`) frame-to-frame, preventing horizontal jitter ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
   Event mapping: Static asset declaration in scene header (`font_family_primary`, `font_family_secondary`, `opentype_features: ["tnum", "kern", "liga"]`) bound to text planar elements.
   Raster/bake note: Render dynamic or scaling text using Multi-channel Signed Distance Fields (MSDF) in the fragment shader or pre-tessellated high-density planar meshes so glyph edges remain razor-sharp across camera zoom events without raster bitmap pixelation.
   Cultural notes: Central European modernist motion design favors Neo-Grotesques (Helvetica, Univers, Aktiv Grotesk); luxury fashion and editorial title design favors high-contrast Didones (Bodoni, Didot) at large display scales (`≥ 10%` frame height); CJK (Chinese, Japanese, Korean) motion typography requires matching weight metrics between Latin glyphs and ideographic Mincho/Gothic character sets.
   Failure modes: Using proportional numerals (`pnum`) on an animated number counter → the entire word/line jitters left and right on every frame as `1` (`narrow`) swaps with `8` (`wide`); using thin hairline fonts (`< 1.5 px` stroke) over moving video → strokes break up into mosquito noise under H.264/H.265 encoding.
   Checklist: [ ] `≤ 2` typeface families (plus optional Monospace for data) [ ] Clear classification contrast or single-superfamily concordance [ ] Minimum stroke width `≥ 1.5 px` at target resolution [ ] `tabular-nums` (`tnum`) active on all animated numbers
   Sources: [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F).

### 3.2 Size vs. Delivery Resolution, Legibility, Tracking, and Leading

Definition: The quantitative scaling of cap height as a percentage of frame height, combined with scale-dependent adjustments to letter-spacing (tracking) and line-spacing (leading).
Why:

- Emotion: Tight, monumental display type commands urgency and authority; generous leading and open tracking evoke calm luxury and airy poise.
- Attention: Ensures glyphs exceed the human visual acuity threshold at the target viewing distance so the eye decodes words as whole shapes (_bouma_) rather than letter-by-letter.
- Production value: Scale-compensated tracking (tightening large headlines, opening small caps/captions) is a hallmark of high-craft typography ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
- Clarity: Prevents ascender/descender collisions across lines and glyph merging under compression.
- Cost: Deterministic percentage-of-frame-height sizing rules allow automatic responsive scaling across `4K`, `1080p`, and `720p` outputs.
  Rules:

1. Specify all type sizes as a percentage of **Frame Height ($H$)** rather than arbitrary points:
   - **Minimum Legible Floor (Legal/Fine Print/HUD metadata)**: Cap height `≥ 2.2% H` (`≥ 24 px` at `1080p`; `≥ 48 px` at `4K`).
   - **Body / Subtitle / Lower-Third Copy**: Cap height `3.5%–5.0% H` (`38–54 px` at `1080p`).
   - **Subheadings / Section Callouts**: Cap height `5.5%–8.5% H` (`60–92 px` at `1080p`).
   - **Hero Display Titles**: Cap height `10.0%–25.0% H` (`108–270 px` at `1080p`) `[OPINION]`.
2. Apply **Inverse Scale Tracking**:
   - Hero Display (`> 10% H`): tighten tracking to `-0.03 em` to `-0.01 em` (`-3%` to `-1%`).
   - Body / Subtitles (`3.5%–5.0% H`): set tracking to `0.00 em` to `+0.02 em`.
   - All-Caps / Small-Caps Labels (`< 4.0% H`): open tracking to `+0.06 em` to `+0.15 em` (`+6%` to `+15%`) ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
3. Apply **Scale-Dependent Leading (Line Height)**:
   - Body / Multi-line Subtitles: `1.25×–1.45×` font size (`125%–145%`).
   - Mixed-case Display Headlines: `1.00×–1.15×` font size.
   - All-Caps Stacked Headlines (no descenders): `0.85×–0.98×` font size ([Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
4. Constrain line measure (column width) to `30–45 characters per line` (`5–8 words`) for motion graphics body blocks, and `≤ 42 characters per line` (`≤ 2 lines`) for timed subtitles ([Netflix Timed Text Style Guide / Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external); [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F)).
   Event mapping: Text element properties `cap_height_norm` (in `[0.022, 0.30]`), `tracking_em` (in `[-0.04, +0.20]`), `leading_ratio` (in `[0.85, 1.50]`), and `max_chars_per_line` (`≤ 45`).
   Raster/bake note: When animating `tracking_em` continuously over time, evaluate glyph positions in floating-point sub-pixel space (`subpixel_positioning = true`) with MSDF/linear texture filtering; snapping animated glyphs to integer pixel coordinates causes severe stepping/shimmering artifacts.
   Cultural notes: Movie trailer end-pages ("billing blocks") use ultra-condensed display type with vertical scale transformation, whereas luxury brand spots often use small all-caps type (`3.0% H`) tracked out to `+0.20 em`–`+0.30 em` at the center of the frame `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Leaving default `0 em` tracking and `1.2×` leading on a massive `20% H` all-caps headline → letters look disconnected and lines float apart; setting all-caps labels at `0 em` tracking → words clump into unreadable rectangular blocks.
   Checklist: [ ] All text cap heights `≥ 2.2%` of frame height [ ] Display type tightened (`-0.03` to `-0.01 em`); all-caps opened (`+0.06` to `+0.15 em`) [ ] Leading set to `1.25–1.45×` for body and `0.85–1.15×` for headlines [ ] Line length `≤ 45 characters` (`≤ 42` for captions) [ ] Sub-pixel positioning enabled for animated type
   Sources: [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F); [Netflix Partner Help Center](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external).

### 3.3 Kinetic Typography and On-Screen Reading Time

Definition: The temporal choreography of text entry, emphasis, transformation, and exit events synchronized to vocal cadence, musical rhythm, or narrative beats.
Why:

- Emotion: Translates vocal prosody (pitch, volume, stress, hesitation) directly into spatial scale, velocity, and weight changes.
- Attention: Sequential word or phrase reveals pace the viewer's eye across the screen and prevent premature reading ahead of the speaker.
- Production value: Hierarchical per-word or per-line staggered animation with masked baselines looks bespoke, whereas unmasked linear opacity fades or per-character rainbow bounces look like amateur presets.
- Clarity: Enforces a stationary reading hold window so motion never compromises comprehension.
- Cost: Reusable event-driven text-unit sequencers (`by_line`, `by_word`, `by_glyph`) generate complex kinetic sequences from declarative timing rules without manual keyframing.
  Rules:

1. Enforce the **Stationary Reading Hold Formula**: after the entrance animation completes (`≥ 90%` settled), hold the text legible for:
   $$T_{\text{hold}} = \max\left(1.5\text{ s},\; 0.3\text{ s} + \frac{N_{\text{words}}}{3.0\text{ words/s}}\right) + 0.4\text{ s} \times N_{\text{other\_moving\_focal\_elements}}$$
   before triggering the exit event `[UNVERIFIED]`.
2. Choose the **Granularity Unit** deliberately:
   - **By-Line** (`stagger = 3–6 frames` at `24–30 fps`): standard for editorial titles, quotes, and multi-line statements.
   - **By-Word** (`stagger = 2–4 frames` or locked to spoken syllable transients): standard for voiceover-synced kinetic typography.
   - **By-Character / Glyph** (`stagger = 1–2 frames`): reserve strictly for short `1–3 word` hero titles (`≤ 18 characters total`); never apply per-character stagger to body sentences (`> 25 characters`), which creates a distracting "typewriter caterpillar" effect `[OPINION]`.
3. Prefer **Masked Baseline Translation** (`Y-offset` from `+100%` line height to `0%` clipped by an invisible horizontal bounding mask per line, `duration = 10–18 frames`, `cubic-out` easing) over simple opacity fades; start opacity at `100%` as the glyph crosses the mask boundary `[OPINION]`.
4. Never animate more than `2` transform channels simultaneously on readable text (e.g., `Y-position + opacity` is clean; `X-position + Y-position + Z-rotation + scale + skew` simultaneously destroys legibility).
   Event mapping: `text_sequence_event(target_text_id, unit="word"|"line"|"char", stagger_frames=3, duration_frames=14, channel=["translate_y", "mask_clip"], easing="cubic_out", hold_duration_s=T_hold)`.
   Raster/bake note: Evaluate kinetic text masks using analytic clip planes or stencil buffers in the raster pass so angled or multi-line baseline reveals maintain crisp anti-aliased edges without extra off-screen render target allocations.
   Cultural notes: Saul Bass and Kyle Cooper title sequences pioneered kinetic typography as psychological prologue; modern short-form vertical video uses center-locked `1–3 word` rapid-replacement kinetic captions (high-retention rapid-word-replacement style) synced to every spoken word with active-word color highlighting (`OKLCH ΔC ≥ +0.12`).
   Failure modes: Starting the exit animation immediately when the entrance ease finishes (`T_hold = 0 s`) → the text is only legible for `3–4 frames` at the apex; applying elastic/bounce per-character animations to a `15-word` paragraph → nauseating visual noise and zero reading comprehension.
   Checklist: [ ] Stationary hold time $T_{\text{hold}}$ satisfies word-count + distraction formula [ ] Per-character animation restricted to `≤ 18 characters` [ ] Line/word staggers set to `2–6 frames` [ ] Maximum `2` simultaneous animated transform channels on readable copy
   Sources: [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F); [Chion, Audio-Vision](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FAudio_vision.html%3Fid%3Dis_HyxAyMkMC); [Lottie v1.0 Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Flottie.github.io%2Flottie-spec%2F1.0%2F).

## 4. Color

### 4.1 Perceptual Color Spaces (OKLab, OKLCH, CIELAB) vs. Display Spaces

Definition: The mathematical parameterization of color for authoring, palette generation, and interpolation using perceptually uniform coordinates—specifically Lightness ($L$), Chroma ($C$), and Hue ($h$) in [OKLCH](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F)—rather than display-encoded sRGB, HSV, or HSL.
Why:

- Emotion: Preserves intended mood and luminance weight when shifting hues across scenes without accidental brightness spikes.
- Attention: Prevents high-luminance hues in non-perceptual spaces (such as sRGB yellow having `Y = 0.928` vs. sRGB blue having `Y = 0.072`) from hijacking visual salience when equal visual weight is intended.
- Production value: Eliminates the muddy gray dead-zones and neon cyan/yellow spikes that plague naive sRGB and HSL color ramps.
- Clarity: Guarantees predictable lightness contrast ($\Delta L$) for legibility across any hue angle.
- Cost: Computing palettes and gradients in OKLab/OKLCH requires only a $3\times 3$ linear matrix + cube-root nonlinearity + $3\times 3$ matrix per [Ottosson (2020)](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F), adding negligible shader or authoring cost.
  Rules:

1. Author all color palettes, procedural color variations, and UI/graphic lightness ramps in **OKLCH** ($L \in [0.0, 1.0]$, $C \in [0.0, 0.37]$, $h \in [0^\circ, 360^\circ)$) rather than HSL or HSV ([Ottosson, Oklab](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F)).
2. Prefer **OKLab / OKLCH** over **CIELAB / CIELCh** ([CIE 1976 Lab\*](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FCIELAB_color_space)) for hue interpolation and saturation scaling: CIELAB exhibits a documented perceptual hue-linearity warp in the blue region ($h \approx 270^\circ\text{–}300^\circ$) where desaturating or blending blue shifts purple/magenta, whereas OKLab maintains constant perceived hue across chroma changes ([Ottosson, Oklab](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F)).
3. When equal visual prominence is required across multiple categorical elements (e.g., data visualization bars, multi-icon sets, secondary graphic shapes), lock $L$ constant within $\pm 0.02$ and $C$ constant within $\pm 0.015$ in OKLCH while varying only $h$ ([Ottosson, Oklab](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F)).
4. Perform gamut mapping from unbounded OKLCH to the target display gamut (sRGB / BT.709 or Display P3) by **reducing Chroma $C$ at constant Lightness $L$ and constant Hue $h$** (binary search along $C$) rather than naïve RGB channel clipping, which corrupts hue and lightness ([Ottosson, Okhsv and Okhsl](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Fcolorpicker%2F)).
   Event mapping: Color-channel events (`color_event`) parameterized in `oklch(L, C, h, alpha)` with interpolation mode set to `space="oklab"` (Cartesian path) or `space="oklch_shorter"` (cylindrical path), converted to linear scene RGB prior to shader lighting evaluation.
   Raster/bake note: Store texture assets in linear-decoded RGB (`VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_SRGB` or `R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT`), execute lighting in linear RGB, and execute procedural color ramps/mixes inside fragment shaders using the analytic OKLab matrix transform.
   Cultural notes: Digital design systems (CSS Color Module Level 4, modern UI/motion frameworks) have standardized on `oklch()` and `oklab()` to replace legacy `hsl()` workflows across web and broadcast graphics.
   Failure modes: Rotating hue in HSL (`hsl(h, 100%, 50%)`) across a sequence → perceived brightness swings wildly by `> 4.5:1` luminance ratio as hue passes through yellow (`L_oklab ≈ 0.97`) vs. blue (`L_oklab ≈ 0.45`); clipping out-of-gamut OKLCH colors via `clamp(rgb, 0, 1)` → saturated blues turn cyan or purple.
   Checklist: [ ] Palettes authored in OKLCH rather than HSL/HSV [ ] Categorical hues share equal $L$ ($\pm 0.02$) and $C$ ($\pm 0.015$) [ ] Out-of-gamut colors mapped by reducing $C$ at constant $L$ and $h$ [ ] Blue gradients interpolated in OKLab/OKLCH rather than CIELAB or sRGB
   Sources: [Ottosson, A perceptual color space for image processing (Oklab)](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F); [Ottosson, Okhsv and Okhsl](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Fcolorpicker%2F); [CIE 1976 Lab\* (CIELAB)](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FCIELAB_color_space).

### 4.2 Color Harmony Systems and Proportional Balance

Definition: Geometric angular relationships on the perceptual hue wheel ($h \in [0^\circ, 360^\circ)$ in OKLCH) paired with strict screen-area proportions that govern palette cohesion and focal tension.
Why:

- Emotion: Monochromatic and analogous chords induce calm, unity, and predictability; complementary and triadic chords induce tension, energy, or playful dynamism ([Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color)).
- Attention: Reserves the minority-area complement (`10%–15%` of screen area) exclusively for the primary focal anchor so the viewer's gaze locks immediately.
- Production value: Proportional area weighting (`60:30:10` or `85:15`) prevents the "circus/fruit-salad" look of equal-area high-chroma colors ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
- Clarity: Assigns distinct semantic roles (Background/Environment, Secondary Structure, Hero Accent) to each chord member.
- Cost: Algorithmic chord generation from a single seed hue `(L₀, C₀, h₀)` allows instant palette derivation across entire sequences.
  Rules:

1. Enforce exact angular offsets in OKLCH and strict **Screen-Area Proportions**:

| Harmony System             | OKLCH Hue Offsets ($\Delta h$)                                | Chroma ($C$) Constraints                                                    | Mandatory Screen-Area Ratio                                       | Target Emotional & Visual Effect                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| :------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------ | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Monochromatic**          | $0^\circ$ ($\pm 5^\circ$)                                     | Vary $C \in [0.01, 0.22]$ and $L \in [0.12, 0.95]$                          | `60%` Dark/Light Base : `30%` Mid-tone : `10%` High-$L$ Highlight | Atmospheric unity, clinical restraint, noir or minimal elegance ([Itten](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color))                                                           |
| **Analogous**              | $h_0,\; h_0 \pm (25^\circ\text{–}40^\circ)$                   | Dominant $C \le 0.12$; Accent $C \in [0.14, 0.24]$                          | `60%` Primary : `30%` Adjacent : `10%` Outer Neighbor             | Naturalistic harmony, organic warmth or cool serenity ([Block](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)) |
| **Complementary**          | $h_0,\; h_0 + 180^\circ$ ($\pm 10^\circ$)                     | Background $C \le 0.08$; Hero Accent $C \ge 0.18$                           | `80%–85%` Dominant/Muted : `15%–20%` Hero Complement              | Maximum focal pop, conflict, cinematic teal-orange separation ([Itten](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color))                                                             |
| **Split-Complementary**    | $h_0,\; h_0 + 150^\circ,\; h_0 + 210^\circ$                   | Base $C \le 0.10$; One split muted ($C \le 0.12$), one vivid ($C \ge 0.18$) | `65%` Base : `25%` Secondary Split : `10%` Vivid Split            | High contrast with sophisticated variety; avoids binary harshness                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **Triadic**                | $h_0,\; h_0 + 120^\circ,\; h_0 + 240^\circ$                   | Desaturate 2 hues ($C \le 0.08$), keep 1 hue vivid ($C \ge 0.18$)           | `70%` Muted Primary : `20%` Muted Secondary : `10%` Vivid Third   | Playful, illustrative, multi-character or categorical clarity                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **Tetradic (Rectangular)** | $h_0,\; h_0 + 60^\circ,\; h_0 + 180^\circ,\; h_0 + 240^\circ$ | At least 2 hues must be near-neutral ($C \le 0.05$)                         | `55%` Base : `25%` Support : `12%` Sub-accent : `8%` Hero Accent  | Complex rich worlds; requires strict lightness hierarchy to avoid chaos                                                                                                                                                                           |

2. Never allow two high-chroma complementary hues ($C \ge 0.16$, $\Delta h \approx 180^\circ$) to occupy equal screen area (`50% : 50%`) or touch at equal lightness ($\Delta L < 0.10$), which triggers uncomfortable retinal border flutter ([Albers, Interaction of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FInteraction_of_Color.html%3Fid%3DA_MYU_XDXfcC)).
   Event mapping: Scene palette dictionary (`palette.base`, `palette.secondary`, `palette.accent`) referenced symbolically by material and planar element color channels; mood transitions execute via a synchronized `palette_lerp_event` across all bound elements.
   Raster/bake note: Pass palette colors as uniform buffer objects (UBOs) to fragment shaders so global harmony adjustments or scene-to-scene color transitions can execute dynamically without re-baking base albedo maps.
   Cultural notes: Hollywood blockbuster grading heavily relies on Complementary Teal-Orange ($h \approx 210^\circ\text{–}230^\circ$ shadows/backgrounds vs. $h \approx 35^\circ\text{–}55^\circ$ skin tones); Bauhaus and De Stijl (Mondrian, Rietveld) employ primary triads anchored by heavy black structural grids and high-key white negative space.
   Failure modes: Giving all three hues in a Triadic palette equal saturation ($C > 0.20$) and equal area (`33%` each) →garish, childish visual chaos where no focal point exists; placing pure saturated red text on a pure saturated cyan background at equal lightness → severe chromostereopsis and unreadable edge vibration.
   Checklist: [ ] Harmony system matches emotional objective [ ] Area distribution follows `60:30:10` or `85:15` rule [ ] At most 1–2 hues exceed high chroma ($C \ge 0.16$) per frame [ ] Zero equal-lightness ($\Delta L < 0.10$) touching complements
   Sources: [Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color); [Albers, Interaction of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FInteraction_of_Color.html%3Fid%3DA_MYU_XDXfcC); [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Ottosson, Oklab](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbottosson.github.io%2Fposts%2Foklab%2F).

### 4.3 Seven Contrasts of Color and Color-to-Emotion Mapping

Definition: Johannes Itten's seven fundamental mechanisms of color differentiation (Value/Light-Dark, Hue, Saturation/Quality, Temperature/Warm-Cool, Complementary, Simultaneous, and Extension/Proportion) and their quantitative mapping to viewer affective response ([Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color)).
Why:

- Emotion: Lightness (Value) and Chroma (Saturation) govern 70%+ of immediate emotional arousal and valence, even more strongly than hue alone `[OPINION]`.
- Attention: Value contrast ($\Delta L$) is processed fastest by the human magnocellular visual pathway, driving immediate pre-attentive eye fixation.
- Production value: Deliberate control of Simultaneous Contrast and Temperature Contrast creates luminous, painterly depth without relying on heavy geometry.
- Clarity: High value contrast guarantees silhouette and typographic legibility under any viewing condition.
- Cost: Adjusting lighting temperature and value contrast achieves dramatic mood shifts at zero geometric or simulation cost.
  Rules:

1. Prioritize **Value Contrast (Light-Dark)** above all other contrasts for focal legibility: maintain an OKLab lightness separation of $\Delta L \ge 0.35$ (or WCAG luminance contrast ratio $\ge 4.5:1$ for text and $\ge 3.0:1$ for key graphical subjects per [WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fcontrast-minimum.html) and [SC 1.4.11](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG21%2FUnderstanding%2Fnon-text-contrast.html)) between the hero subject and its immediate background.
2. Exploit **Warm-Cool (Temperature) Depth Contrast**: warm hues in OKLCH ($h \in [15^\circ, 85^\circ]$, red-orange-yellow) perceptually advance toward the viewer, while cool hues ($h \in [195^\circ, 275^\circ]$, cyan-blue) recede; assign warm accents to foreground focal elements and cool desaturated tones ($C \le 0.08$) to deep background planes ([Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color); [Birn, Digital Lighting and Rendering](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2F3drender.com%2Flight%2Findex.html)).
3. Compensate for **Simultaneous Contrast** (Albers effect): a neutral gray or muted element surrounded by a large dark field appears `10%–18%` lighter than the exact same color surrounded by a light field, and shifts in hue toward the complement of its background ([Albers, Interaction of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FInteraction_of_Color.html%3Fid%3DA_MYU_XDXfcC)). Offset the foreground swatch by $\Delta L = \pm 0.05$ and $\Delta h = 5^\circ\text{–}12^\circ$ toward the background hue when two elements across different backgrounds must appear identical to the viewer.
4. Apply the **Quantitative Color-to-Emotion Matrix**:

| Target Affective State             | OKLab Lightness ($L$)                       | OKLCH Chroma ($C$)                              | Dominant Hue Band ($h$)                                               | Value Contrast ($\Delta L$)                  | Psychological / Physiological Mechanism                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| :--------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :---------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Urgency / Danger / Aggression**  | Low–Mid ($0.20\text{–}0.55$)                | High ($0.20\text{–}0.30$)                       | Red–Orange ($20^\circ\text{–}45^\circ$)                               | Extreme ($\Delta L \ge 0.65$)                | High autonomic arousal; biological warning signal ([Itten](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color))                                                                             |
| **Joy / Optimism / Playfulness**   | High ($0.72\text{–}0.92$)                   | Mid–High ($0.14\text{–}0.24$)                   | Warm Yellow–Peach–Mint ($65^\circ\text{–}150^\circ$)                  | Moderate ($\Delta L \in [0.35, 0.55]$)       | High photopic luminance mimics daylight warmth                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Calm / Trust / Institutional**   | Mid–High ($0.45\text{–}0.85$)               | Low–Mid ($0.04\text{–}0.12$)                    | Cool Blue–Teal ($210^\circ\text{–}260^\circ$)                         | Soft–Moderate ($\Delta L \in [0.30, 0.50]$)  | Short-wavelength dominance lowers perceived visual clutter                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **Melancholy / Isolation / Grief** | Low–Mid ($0.18\text{–}0.48$)                | Very Low ($0.01\text{–}0.06$)                   | Slate Blue–Cyan ($200^\circ\text{–}250^\circ$)                        | Compressed ($\Delta L \le 0.30$, except rim) | Chroma deprivation + compressed dynamic range mute affect ([Block](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)) |
| **Mystery / Uncanny / Cyberpunk**  | Low ($0.10\text{–}0.35$)                    | High Accents ($0.22\text{–}0.32$)               | Violet–Magenta–Acid Green ($300^\circ\text{–}350^\circ$, $135^\circ$) | High localized ($\Delta L \ge 0.60$)         | Non-naturalistic spectral combinations rare in daylight                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Luxury / Prestige / Gravity**    | Deep ($0.08\text{–}0.22$) or High ($0.90+$) | Achromatic ($0.00\text{–}0.03$) + Gold ($0.10$) | Neutral + Warm Amber ($70^\circ\text{–}85^\circ$)                     | Crisp ($\Delta L \ge 0.55$)                  | Extreme restraint in chroma signals exclusivity                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Event mapping: Keyframed or event-driven shifts in `scene_contrast_profile` (`lightness_floor`, `lightness_ceiling`, `chroma_multiplier`, `temp_kelvin_offset`) triggered at narrative turning points.
Raster/bake note: Simultaneous contrast and warm-cool depth operate purely via pixel values; ensure baked lightmaps do not wash out local value contrast ($\Delta L \ge 0.35$) around the hero subject.
Cultural notes: White signifies purity and weddings in Western contexts but mourning and funerals in traditional East Asian and South Asian contexts; Red signifies danger/stop in Western industrial contexts but prosperity, celebration, and good fortune in Chinese and Vietnamese cultural contexts; Yellow/Gold signifies royalty and sacredness in East/Southeast Asia (`[OPINION]`; cross-cultural semiotics must be audited against the target region in §1.1).
Failure modes: Relying solely on Hue Contrast ($\Delta h = 120^\circ$) while leaving Lightness identical ($\Delta L = 0.0$) → image flattens completely into an indistinct mush for viewers with color-vision deficiency and fails the grayscale check; using high-chroma warm reds in the background and cool muted blues on the hero subject → background visually jumps in front of the subject.
Checklist: [ ] Hero subject has $\Delta L \ge 0.35$ against immediate background [ ] Foreground warm / background cool depth separation applied (or intentionally inverted with high $\Delta L$) [ ] Simultaneous contrast compensated when matching swatches across different backgrounds [ ] Cross-cultural color associations verified against target region
Sources: [Itten, The Art of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.goodreads.com%2Fen%2Fbook%2Fshow%2F967310.The_Art_of_Color); [Albers, Interaction of Color](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FInteraction_of_Color.html%3Fid%3DA_MYU_XDXfcC); [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fcontrast-minimum.html).

### 4.4 Global Palettes, Per-Scene Palettes, and Color Scripts

Definition: A timeline-mapped visual score (color script) that plots the progression of dominant, secondary, and accent OKLCH swatches across every scene from `t = 0` to `t = end` to mirror the narrative and emotional arc.
Why:

- Emotion: Visualizes the macro-emotional journey at a glance (e.g., progressing from desaturated cold compression in Act I to warm high-contrast expansion in Act III) ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
- Attention: Uses inter-scene palette shifts as macro-pattern interrupts that refresh viewer attention at structural transitions.
- Production value: Prevents individual shots from looking like they were designed by unconnected artists in isolation.
- Clarity: Uses consistent color leitmotifs (assigning a reserved signature hue to a specific character, concept, or state) so the viewer tracks narrative threads effortlessly.
- Cost: Locking the color script before lighting and shader authoring avoids re-grading and re-baking scenes that clash when edited together.
  Rules:

1. Define a **Global Master Palette** of `5–7` core swatches (1 Dark Neutral $L \in [0.08, 0.16]$, 1 Light Neutral $L \in [0.90, 0.98]$, 1 Mid Neutral, 2 Primary Brand/World Hues, and 1–2 Reserved Hero Accents) that bounds the entire production `[OPINION]`.
2. Derive **Per-Scene Palettes** as constrained subsets (`3–4` active swatches per scene) of the Global Master Palette, shifting global lightness $L$, chroma $C$, and temperature bias across scenes to match the **Visual Intensity Curve** ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
3. Reserve the **Climax / CTA Swatch** (the highest-chroma or highest-contrast hue in the global palette, e.g., $C \ge 0.24$) so it appears at `< 2%` screen area in Acts I–II and expands to `15%–25%` screen area only at the narrative climax or Call to Action ([Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)).
4. Plot the **Color Script Strip** as a horizontal bar chart where bar width is proportional to scene duration ($t_{\text{scene}} / t_{\text{total}}$) and inspect adjacent scene boundaries: adjacent scenes within the same act should shift hue by $\Delta h \le 30^\circ$, whereas act boundaries or plot reversals should shift by $\Delta h \ge 90^\circ$ or $\Delta L \ge 0.30$ `[OPINION]`.
   Event mapping: Top-level `color_script_track` containing sequential `scene_palette_cue(start_time, duration, bg_oklch, mid_oklch, accent_oklch, ambient_light_kelvin)` events that drive scene uniforms.
   Raster/bake note: When baking static lightmaps for 3D scenes that undergo dynamic color-script shifts within the same shot, bake neutral white (`C = 0`) direct/indirect lightmaps into separate RGB channels (e.g., Red = Key, Green = Fill, Blue = Rim/Bounce) and tint them dynamically in the fragment shader via the `scene_palette_cue` uniform.
   Cultural notes: Feature animation studios (feature animation color-script workflows) popularized painted horizontal color scripts to govern lighting progression across 90-minute features; commercial motion studios use modular 8–12 frame digital color scripts for 30–60 second spots `[OPINION]`.
   Failure modes: Using the maximum-chroma climax color in Scene 1 at full saturation → visual nowhere left to go at the climax; changing palettes randomly every shot without narrative motivation → disjointed montage that confuses spatial/temporal continuity.
   Checklist: [ ] Global Master Palette bounded to `5–7` core swatches [ ] Per-scene palettes restricted to `3–4` active swatches [ ] Climax/CTA accent hue held back (`< 2%` area) until climax [ ] Color script strip proportional to scene durations verified
   Sources: [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Birn, Digital Lighting and Rendering](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2F3drender.com%2Flight%2Findex.html).

### 4.5 Gradients, Interpolation Spaces, Banding Prevention, and Dithering

Definition: Continuous spatial transitions between two or more color stops (linear, radial, conic/angular, or 2D/3D freeform mesh gradients) evaluated in a perceptually uniform color space and quantized with noise dithering to eliminate Mach banding.
Why:
