| **Classical Three-Act Narrative**        | **`0%–22%`** (Hook at `0%–5%`; Inciting Incident at `12%–18%`)                                           | **`22%–75%`** (Rising complications; Midpoint reversal at `50%`; Darkest/Peak tension at `72%`)                              | **`75%–100%`** (Climax peak at **`78%–86%`**; Resolution/CTA at `86%–100%`)                                 | Brand films, animations, short films, trailers (`60 s – 15 min`) ([Block](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152)) |
| **Problem → Agitate → Solution (`PAS`)** | **`0%–20%` (Problem)**: Visceral depiction of friction/pain point (desaturated/compressed visual state). | **`20%–40%` (Agitate)**: Escalating cost of failure → **`40%` Pivot**: Product/Solution reveal with palette/light expansion. | **`40%–85%` (Solution Proof)**: 3 crisp visual demonstrations → **`85%–100%` (CTA)**: Single action lockup. | Product explainers, commercial spots, SaaS motion graphics (`15 s – 90 s`) `[OPINION]`                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Kishōtenketsu (4-Act Twist)**          | **`Ki` (`0%–25%`)**: Establish world/motif.                                                              | **`Shō` (`25%–50%`)**: Develop motif without conflict → **`Ten` (`50%–80%`)**: Unexpected orthogonal perspective twist.      | **`Ketsu` (`80%–100%`)**: Harmonious synthesis of `Ki` and `Ten`.                                           | Poetic/abstract motion art, luxury branding, East Asian storytelling `[OPINION]`                                                                                                                                                                             |

Event mapping: Top-level structural timeline `narrative_arc_manifest(framework="three_act"|"pas"|"kishotenketsu", climax_normalized_time=0.82, intensity_curve=[(0.0, 0.6), (0.15, 0.3), (0.50, 0.65), (0.82, 1.0), (0.95, 0.25)])`.
Raster/bake note: Allocate highest lightmap texel density, highest particle count, and active dynamic shadow casters to the **Climax (`75%–88%` runtime)** and **Hook (`0%–10%` runtime)** scenes.
Cultural notes: Western dramaturgy (Aristotle, Syd Field, Bruce Block) centers on binary conflict and resolution; East Asian _Kishōtenketsu_ generates emotional insight through juxtaposition and perspective shift (_Ten_) without requiring an antagonist.
Failure modes: Putting maximum visual chaos (`100%` chroma, Dutch angles, explosions, rapid cuts) at `t = 10%` and maintaining it identically until `t = 100%` → complete numbness ("when everything is loud, nothing is loud"); letting the Climax occur at `t = 99%` with zero resolution hold → abrupt, unsatisfying cutoff.
Checklist: [ ] Structural framework (`Three-Act`, `PAS`, or `Kishōtenketsu`) explicitly mapped to timeline percentages [ ] Visual Intensity Curve (contrast, angle, chroma, ASL, motion speed) peaks at the exact narrative Climax (`75%–88%` runtime) [ ] Solution/Pivot in `PAS` accompanied by an immediate lighting/palette expansion [ ] Post-climax resolution (`final 12%–15%`) drops visual intensity to allow emotional assimilation
Sources: [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152).

### 18.2 Information Order, Cognitive Load Budgeting, Clarity of Message, and Call to Action (CTA)

Definition: The sequencing of visual and verbal propositions so that working-memory load never exceeds human cognitive capacity (`≤ 3–4` chunks per scene) and culminates in an unmistakable, frictionless Call to Action.
Why:

- Emotion: Cognitive fluency—effortlessly understanding a complex mechanism through clear visual progression—produces a powerful feeling of aesthetic pleasure and trust in the user's message.
- Attention: Presenting one concept per shot prevents split-attention exhaustion.
- Production value: Confident pacing that gives each idea space to breathe reads as executive authority, whereas cramming 5 bullet points, 3 charts, and a voiceover onto one slide reads as amateur corporate panic.
- Clarity: Ensures `100%` of viewers can state the video's core point and next step in a single sentence after one viewing.
- Cost: Cutting redundant secondary propositions reduces asset count and runtime by `25%–40%`.
  Rules:

1. **The One-Proposition-Per-Shot Law (Cognitive Load Budget)**:
   - Assign **at most `1` primary new informational concept** to any single shot or `3–5 second` visual window `[OPINION]`.
   - Cap simultaneous distinct visual chunks on screen at **`≤ 3` items** (e.g., `3` icon cards, `3` comparison bars, or `1` 3D assembly split into `3` labeled modules). If a process has `9` steps, group them into **`3` macro-stages of `3` sub-steps**, revealing them progressively rather than all `9` at once.
2. **Given-Before-New Information Ordering**:
   - Always anchor a new concept to the visual element established in the immediately preceding shot (**Visual Bridging**): e.g., Shot A establishes the whole device → Shot B zooms into the specific sensor module inside that device → Shot C shows the data stream emitted by that sensor. Never cut to an un-anchored abstract diagram before showing where it lives in the physical/conceptual whole `[OPINION]`.
3. **Show First, Label Second (`6–12 Frame` Visual Lead)**:
   - When introducing a new visual state or product feature, let the **visual transformation begin `6–12 frames` (`0.25–0.50 s`) BEFORE** the narrator speaks its name or the text label pops up. This triggers natural curiosity ("what is that?") which the label immediately satisfies, rather than announcing a spoiler label before the eye sees anything ([Chion, Audio-Vision](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FAudio_vision.html%3Fid%3Dis_HyxAyMkMC)).
4. **Call to Action (CTA) Architecture**:
   - **Singular Action Rule**: Include **exactly `1` Call to Action** in the final `10%–15%` of runtime (`minimum duration = 2.5–4.0 seconds` stationary hold). Asking the viewer to simultaneously "Subscribe, comment, visit our website, download the app, and use code X" reduces conversion by splitting decision focus `[OPINION]`.
   - **Visual Isolation**: During the CTA hold, reduce background luminance contrast to $\Delta L \le 0.15$ and halt all non-essential background motion so `100%` of scene contrast ($\Delta L \ge 0.65$) and the reserved Climax/CTA Accent Hue (§4.4) lock onto the single CTA text/button/URL (`≤ 6 words`).
     Event mapping: `cta_lockup_event(start_frame=total_frames - 90, duration_frames=90, text="Single Action Command", bg_contrast_compression=0.80, hero_accent_oklch=palette.climax_accent, secondary_motion_freeze=true)`.
     Raster/bake note: During high-cognitive-load diagram or CTA windows, use a post-process depth-of-field + exposure-dimming shader pass (`exposure_ev = -1.2`, `coc_px = 12.0`) on the 3D background layer so overlying 2D/3D callout typography achieves effortless `≥ 7:1` contrast without needing an opaque black box.
     Cultural notes: Direct-response advertising places a soft mid-roll CTA cue at `50%` runtime and a hard CTA lockup at `90%`; prestige brand and theatrical pieces omit explicit verbal CTAs entirely, ending on a solitary `3.0-second` iconic visual symbol or sonic mnemonic logo `[OPINION]`.
     Failure modes: Displaying a 5-column financial table with 20 numbers for `2.5 seconds` while the narrator talks about something else → zero numbers are read; fading out the CTA card after only `0.8 seconds` → viewer cannot read or act on the URL/command in time.
     Checklist: [ ] Maximum `1` new proposition per shot and `≤ 3` distinct visual chunks on screen simultaneously [ ] Information progresses from known whole → specific part (Given-Before-New) [ ] Visual reveal leads spoken/text label by `6–12 frames` [ ] Exactly `1` singular CTA (`≤ 6 words`) held stationary for `≥ 2.5–4.0 seconds` with isolated background contrast
     Sources: [Block, The Visual Story](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.routledge.com%2FThe-Visual-Story-Creating-the-Visual-Structure-of-Film-TV-and-Digital-Media%2FBlock%2Fp%2Fbook%2F9781138014152); [Chion, Audio-Vision](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%2Fabout%2FAudio_vision.html%3Fid%3Dis_HyxAyMkMC); [Bringhurst, The Elements of Typographic Style](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thriftbooks.com%2Fw%2Fthe-elements-of-typographic-style_robert-bringhurst%2F278779%2F).

## 19. Accessibility

### 19.1 Captions, Luminance Contrast Ratios, and Minimum On-Screen Read Time

Definition: The normative legibility, luminance contrast, reading-rate, and timed-text synchronization rules ensuring all visual and auditory information is accessible to users with hearing or vision impairments ([WCAG 2.2](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG22%2F); [Netflix Timed Text Style Guide / Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external)).
Why:

- Emotion: Clean, well-timed captions that respect shot changes preserve dramatic timing and comedy punchlines for deaf, hard-of-hearing, and sound-off viewers.
- Attention: High luminance contrast (`≥ 4.5:1` to `7.0:1`) eliminates squinting and eye fatigue across mobile and bright-ambient viewing conditions.
- Production value: Captions that snap cleanly across cut boundaries without straddling a cut by `3 frames` mark professional broadcast post-production ([Netflix Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external)).
- Clarity: Guarantees `100%` text and data legibility across color-vision deficiencies (protanopia, deuteranopia, tritanopia) and low-vision displays.
- Cost: Enforcing WCAG contrast formulas and CPS limits programmatically in the scene validator catches legibility violations in milliseconds before rendering.
  Rules:

1. **WCAG 2.2 Luminance Contrast Thresholds ([WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fcontrast-minimum.html) / [SC 1.4.6](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG22%2F) / [SC 1.4.11](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG21%2FUnderstanding%2Fnon-text-contrast.html))**:
   - Compute relative luminance in linear sRGB ($Y = 0.2126 R_{\text{lin}} + 0.7152 G_{\text{lin}} + 0.0722 B_{\text{lin}}$ per [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709)) and contrast ratio $C_{\text{ratio}} = \frac{Y_{\text{lighter}} + 0.05}{Y_{\text{darker}} + 0.05}$:
     - **Body / Caption Text (`< 4.5% H`)**: Enforce **$C\_{\text{ratio}} \ge 4.5 : 1$** minimum (WCAG AA) and target **`≥ 7.0 : 1`** (WCAG AAA) because moving video backgrounds degrade effective legibility compared to static web pages!
     - **Large Display Headlines (`≥ 5.0% H` bold or `≥ 6.5% H` regular)**: Enforce **$C\_{\text{ratio}} \ge 3.0 : 1$** minimum (WCAG AA) and target **`≥ 4.5 : 1`**.
     - **Essential Graphical Icons, Chart Lines, & UI Boundaries ([SC 1.4.11](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG21%2FUnderstanding%2Fnon-text-contrast.html))**: Enforce **$C\_{\text{ratio}} \ge 3.0 : 1$** against adjacent background pixels.
   - When placing white text (`Y = 1.0`) over bright or variable video footage, always back the text with a **Semi-Transparent Dark Pill Box (`opacity = 65%–80%` black, corner radius `0.8% H`, padding `0.8% H`)**, an outer dark stroke (`width = 6%–10%` of font size), or a localized soft gradient scrim so $C_{\text{ratio}} \ge 7.0:1$ is guaranteed regardless of background motion.
2. **Color-Vision Deficiency (CVD) Redundant Coding Rule ([WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG22%2F))**:
   - Never rely on hue alone (e.g., Red vs. Green lines/dots) to distinguish data series or status states. Pair every hue difference with a **Lightness separation ($\Delta L \ge 0.25$ in OKLab)** AND a **Secondary Geometric Cue** (distinct dash pattern, icon shape, direct text label, or texture hatching).
3. **Timed-Text / Caption Synchronization & Reading Speed ([Netflix Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external))**:
   - **Reading Speed Ceiling**: `≤ 17 characters per second (CPS)` for adult programming (`≤ 13 CPS` for children's content).
   - **Minimum & Maximum Event Duration**: Every caption event must stay on screen for **`≥ 5/6 second` (`833 ms`, `20 frames` @ `24 fps`)** and **`≤ 7.0 seconds`**.
   - **Line Constraints**: Maximum **`2 lines`** per caption card, **`≤ 42 characters per line`**, broken at natural syntactic clause boundaries (never split a determiner/adjective from its noun across lines).
   - **Shot-Cut Alignment Gap**: Maintain a minimum **`2-frame` gap** between consecutive caption cards. If a speech phrase ends within `1–11 frames` of a visual shot cut, **snap the caption's out-point directly to the cut frame** (never let a caption hang `3 frames` over a cut into a new scene or stop `3 frames` before a cut) ([Netflix Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external)).
     Event mapping: `caption_track_validator(min_contrast_ratio=7.0, max_cps=17.0, min_duration_ms=833, max_duration_ms=7000, max_lines=2, max_chars_per_line=42, snap_to_cut_tolerance_frames=11)`.
     Raster/bake note: Sample the underlying rendered framebuffer inside each text element's bounding box via a compute reduction pass (finding `min_Y` and `max_Y` behind the glyphs) to verify $C_{\text{ratio}} \ge 4.5:1$ automatically on every rendered frame, or dynamically adjust the backing scrim opacity channel to maintain target contrast.
     Cultural notes: Closed Captions (`.srt` / `.vtt` / `CEA-708`) must include non-speech atmospheric and narrative sound descriptors in brackets (`[ominous synth pulse]`, `[glass shatters]`) for deaf and hard-of-hearing parity, whereas Open "Burned-In" Social Subtitles transcribe only spoken words.
     Failure modes: Placing pure white un-stroked text over a white shirt or bright sky in a moving shot → text vanishes completely for `2 seconds`; letting a subtitle card linger `4 frames` into the next shot after a hard cut → triggers a visual double-take that makes the cut feel broken.
     Checklist: [ ] Text luminance contrast ratio $\ge 4.5:1$ (`≥ 7.0:1` target over moving video via scrim/pill/stroke) [ ] Non-text icons and chart elements achieve $\ge 3.0:1$ contrast and use redundant non-hue cues [ ] Caption reading speed `≤ 17 CPS`, duration `0.833–7.0 s`, `≤ 2 lines`, `≤ 42 chars/line` [ ] Captions within `11 frames` of a shot cut are snapped cleanly to the cut boundary
     Sources: [WCAG 2.2 Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG22%2F); [WCAG 2.2 Understanding SC 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum)](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fcontrast-minimum.html); [WCAG 2.1 Understanding SC 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG21%2FUnderstanding%2Fnon-text-contrast.html); [Netflix Partner Help Center](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external).

### 19.2 Photosensitive Seizure / Flash Limits (WCAG SC 2.3.1) and Reduced Motion Alternatives

Definition: The strict photic flash frequency, luminance delta, saturated-red transition, and screen-area limits required to prevent photosensitive epileptic seizures ([WCAG 2.2 SC 2.3.1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fthree-flashes-or-below-threshold.html); [ITU-R BT.1702](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG20-TECHS%2FG15)), paired with vestibular-safe reduced-motion choreography.
Why:

- Emotion: Protects viewers from physical discomfort, migraines, vertigo, and life-threatening photosensitive seizures.
- Attention: Replaces cheap, dangerous strobe-light flashing with safe localized luminance pulses (`< 25%` screen area or `< 3 Hz`).
- Production value: Passing automated Harding / WCAG 2.3.1 PSE (Photosensitive Epilepsy) broadcast QC is a mandatory legal gate for television, streaming, and game distribution globally.
- Clarity: Eliminates disorienting full-screen flicker and violent camera roll that obscure visual comprehension.
- Cost: Auditing flash frequency (`≤ 3 flashes/s`) and relative luminance delta ($\Delta Y < 0.10$) during event scheduling prevents broadcast master rejection and re-rendering.
  Rules:

1. **WCAG 2.2 SC 2.3.1 & ITU-R BT.1702 General Flash Threshold (Hard Safety Law)**:
   - Never allow any sequence to violate all three of the **General Flash Criteria** simultaneously ([WCAG 2.2 SC 2.3.1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fthree-flashes-or-below-threshold.html)):
     1. **Frequency Limit**: More than **`3 flashes`** (6 opposing light-dark transitions) within any rolling **`1.0-second` window** (`f > 3.0 Hz`; human cortical seizure sensitivity peaks sharply in the `10–25 Hz` band, i.e., alternating every `1–2 frames` at `24–60 fps`).
     2. **Luminance Delta Limit**: A pair of opposing transitions where the relative luminance $Y \in [0.0, 1.0]$ changes by **`≥ 10%` ($\Delta Y \ge 0.10$)** AND the darker image's relative luminance is **`< 0.80`**.
     3. **Screen Area Limit**: The flashing pixels occupy a combined contiguous or proximate area exceeding **`25%` of any `10°` visual field viewport** (conservatively enforced in production as **`≤ 10%–20%` of total frame area**).
2. **The Saturated Red Flash Prohibition ([WCAG 2.2 SC 2.3.1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fthree-flashes-or-below-threshold.html))**:
   - The human retina and visual cortex are uniquely hypersensitive to flashing deep red light (`600–700 nm`), even when luminance $Y$ remains constant!
   - Define a **Saturated Red** pixel as $\frac{R_{\text{lin}}}{R_{\text{lin}} + G_{\text{lin}} + B_{\text{lin}}} \ge 0.80$, and forbid more than **`3` transitions per second** where $\Delta(R_{\text{lin}} - G_{\text{lin}} - B_{\text{lin}}) \times 320 > 20$ across `> 10%` of frame area.
3. **Safe Impact Flash Technique**:
   - When an action anime hit, lightning strike, or trailer impact requires an energetic flash, keep it 100% PSE-compliant by either: (a) executing **at most `1` or `2` flashes** within any `1.0-second` window (e.g., `1 frame` white at `35%` opacity → `6 frame` smooth exponential decay to normal, never alternating `black-white-black-white-black-white` on consecutive frames), OR (b) confining the high-contrast flash strictly to a localized rim/spark element covering **`< 10%` of total screen area**.
4. **Vestibular & Reduced-Motion Profile ([WCAG 2.2 SC 2.3.3](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG22%2F))**:
   - When authoring a `reduced_motion` variant (or avoiding vestibular nausea in general audiences), cap continuous full-screen optical zoom/parallax translation to `< 20%` screen area or replace `6-frame` whip pans / Z-axis tunnel zooms with clean `12–18 frame` cross-dissolves and subtle `±3%` planar slides.
     Event mapping: Runtime safety gate `pse_flash_validator(max_flashes_per_sec=3.0, max_delta_relative_luma=0.10, max_flashing_screen_area_pct=15.0, block_saturated_red_alternation=true)`.
     Raster/bake note: Include a compute-shader temporal luminance & red-saturation histogram monitor over a rolling `1.0-second` ring buffer (`24–60 frames`) broken into an `8×8` screen grid; if any `2×2` sub-block accumulates `> 3` opposing transitions with $\Delta Y \ge 0.10$ in `1.0 s`, automatically clamp temporal luminance contrast or flag the exact timecode.
     Cultural notes: Following the 1997 Japanese broadcast incident (_Pokémon_ Episode 38, which alternated full-screen saturated red and blue at `12 Hz` and hospitalized 685 viewers), Japan's NAB/NHK and the UK's Ofcom (Harding Test) enacted strict automated PSE rejection gates now universally enforced by Netflix, YouTube, and broadcast networks ([WCAG G15 / ITU-R BT.1702](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG20-TECHS%2FG15)).
     Failure modes: Animating a strobe effect by toggling background visibility `ON / OFF` every `2 frames` at `24 fps` (`6 flashes/second` at `100%` screen area, $\Delta Y = 1.0$) → catastrophic WCAG 2.3.1 violation and severe seizure hazard; alternating full-screen saturated Red and Blue cards rapidly → triggers red-flash seizure hazard even at equal luminance.
     Checklist: [ ] Zero sequences exceed `3 flashes/second` with $\Delta Y \ge 0.10$ across `> 10%–25%` of frame area [ ] Zero rapid alternations involving Saturated Red ($R / (R+G+B) \ge 0.80$) [ ] Impact flashes use a single decayed pulse (`1f` peak + `6f` exponential tail) rather than multi-frame strobing [ ] Camera roll and violent Z-axis parallax bounded to prevent vestibular motion sickness
     Sources: [WCAG 2.2 Understanding SC 2.3.1: Three Flashes or Below Threshold](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fthree-flashes-or-below-threshold.html); [W3C Technique G15 / ITU-R BT.1702](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FTR%2FWCAG20-TECHS%2FG15); [WCAG 2.2 Understanding SC 2.3.2 Three Flashes](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.w3.org%2FWAI%2FWCAG22%2FUnderstanding%2Fthree-flashes.html).

## 20. Delivery

### 20.1 Codecs, Bitrates, Frame Rates, Resolutions, Aspect Ratios, and Color Metadata Tags

Definition: The compression architecture, chroma subsampling, bit depth, GOP structure, bitrate allocation, and container color-space signaling (`NCLC` / `VUI` atoms) used to encode rendered frames into archival masters and distribution deliverables ([ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709); [ITU-R BT.2100](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2100); [YouTube Encoding Spec](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en)).
Why:

- Emotion: Sufficient bitrate and 10-bit precision preserve subtle atmospheric gradients, film grain, and dark shadow nuance so the emotional mood survives distribution.
- Attention: Prevents macroblock breakup during high-velocity particle bursts or whip transitions from distracting the viewer's eye.
- Production value: Explicit NCLC color tagging (`1-1-1` for SDR BT.709) prevents the dreaded washed-out gamma shift across browsers and operating systems.
- Clarity: High-profile CABAC encoding with closed GOPs ensures razor-sharp typographic edges across every frame.
- Cost: Two-tier delivery (10-bit/12-bit mezzanine master + target-bitrate distribution encodes) avoids ever having to re-render the 3D/2D scene for new platforms.
  Rules:

1. **Two-Tier Encoding Architecture (Mezzanine Master vs. Distribution Deliverable)**:
   - **Tier 1 — Archival / Mezzanine Master**: Always render from the GPU pipeline first to an intra-frame mezzanine master (**10-bit 4:2:2 Intra-Frame Mezzanine (ProRes 422 HQ / DNxHR HQX)** `10-bit 4:2:2` at `~220 Mbps` for `1080p24` / `~880 Mbps` for `4K24`, or **ProRes 4444** `12-bit 4:4:4 + Alpha` when transparency or crisp red-on-black vector edges require uncompressed chroma) or `16-bit` OpenEXR image sequence ([Netflix Delivery Specs](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external)).
   - **Tier 2 — Web / Social / Client Distribution (`.mp4` Container)**: Transcode from the Mezzanine Master using **2-Pass VBR** (with `max_bitrate = 1.5 × target_bitrate`) or **CRF `15–18`** (`H.264 / AVC High Profile`, `H.265 / HEVC Main10`, or `AV1`) with **progressive scan (never interlaced)**, **`Closed GOP` (`keyframe interval = fps / 2` or `1.0–2.0 seconds`)**, **`2` consecutive B-frames**, and **`CABAC` entropy coding** ([YouTube Upload Encoding Settings](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en)).
2. **The 4:2:0 Chroma Subsampling Typography Defense**:
   - All standard consumer web video (`H.264 8-bit 4:2:0`) discards **`75%` of color (Cb/Cr) resolution** (storing only 1 color sample per `2×2` block of luma pixels). If small high-chroma red or blue text (`stroke < 3 px`) is placed directly on a dark background with equal luminance ($\Delta Y \approx 0$), `4:2:0` subsampling smears the edges into unreadable staircase blocks.
   - **Rule**: Ensure all fine edges and small typography (`< 4% H`) carry **Luminance Contrast ($\Delta Y \ge 0.35$)** rather than pure chroma contrast, or keep stroke widths `≥ 2.5–3.0 px` on even pixel coordinates.
3. **Master Bitrate & Parameter Matrix**:

| Resolution & Frame Rate                                   | Aspect Ratio    | H.264 (AVC) Target Bitrate (SDR `8-bit`)                                                      | H.265 (HEVC `10-bit`) / AV1 Target Bitrate                           | Audio Stream Codec & Bitrate                     |
| :-------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------- |
| **`1080p` (`1920×1080` or `1080×1920`) @ `24/25/30 fps`** | `16:9` / `9:16` | **`12–20 Mbps`** (YouTube min `8–10 Mbps`; increase to `20–25 Mbps` if heavy grain/particles) | **`8–14 Mbps`** (`Main10` profile eliminates 8-bit gradient banding) | `AAC-LC`, `48 kHz`, `320 kbps` (or `≥ 256 kbps`) |
| **`1080p` @ `60 fps`**                                    | `16:9` / `9:16` | **`18–30 Mbps`** (YouTube min `12 Mbps`)                                                      | **`12–20 Mbps`**                                                     | `AAC-LC`, `48 kHz`, `320 kbps`                   |
| **`1440p` (`2560×1440`) @ `24/30 fps` (`60 fps`)**        | `16:9`          | **`24–32 Mbps`** (`36–48 Mbps` @ `60 fps`)                                                    | **`16–24 Mbps`** (`24–32 Mbps` @ `60 fps`)                           | `AAC-LC`, `48 kHz`, `320 kbps`                   |
| **`4K UHD` (`3840×2160`) @ `24/25/30 fps`**               | `16:9`          | **`40–65 Mbps`** (YouTube rec `35–45 Mbps` SDR, `44–56 Mbps` HDR)                             | **`25–45 Mbps`** (`Main10` mandatory for HDR10/HLG)                  | `AAC-LC`, `48 kHz`, `320–512 kbps`               |
| **`4K UHD` @ `60 fps`**                                   | `16:9`          | **`60–85 Mbps`** (YouTube rec `53–68 Mbps` SDR, `66–85 Mbps` HDR)                             | **`40–60 Mbps`**                                                     | `AAC-LC`, `48 kHz`, `320–512 kbps`               |

4. **Mandatory Container Metadata Signaling**:
   - Explicitly write `colr` / `VUI` flags into every `.mp4` / `.mov` header: for SDR HD/4K, set **`color_primaries = 1 (bt709)`, `color_trc = 1 (bt709)`, `colorspace = 1 (bt709)`, `color_range = tv / limited (16–235 in 8-bit, 64–940 in 10-bit)`** (never export unmarked `Full Range 0–255` YUV in H.264 unless a specialized pipeline explicitly requests it, as standard hardware video decoders map `16–235` to `0.0–1.0` and will crush the bottom/top `6%` of an unmarked Full Range stream into clipped black and white) ([ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709); [YouTube Encoding Settings](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en)).
   - Place the `.mp4` `moov` atom at the **front of the file (`+faststart`)** so web playback starts instantaneously without downloading the entire file.
     Event mapping: Final encoder specification `delivery_encode_config(container="mp4", faststart=true, video_codec="h264_high"|"hevc_main10", pixel_format="yuv420p"|"yuv420p10le", color_range="tv_limited", nclc=[1,1,1], gop_frames=12, b_frames=2, bitrate_mbps=20.0, audio_codec="aac_lc", audio_kbps=320)`.
     Raster/bake note: Convert final tone-mapped linear RGB `[0.0, 1.0]` to `BT.709` nonlinear `R'G'B'`, apply `±1.0 LSB` blue-noise dithering, transform via the exact `BT.709` luma-chroma matrix ($Y' = 0.2126 R' + 0.7152 G' + 0.0722 B'$), and scale to legal `16–235` (`8-bit`) or `64–940` (`10-bit`) code values before handing off to the hardware encoder.
     Cultural notes: PAL territories (Europe, UK, Australia, China, much of Asia) broadcast natively at **`25.00 fps` / `50.00 fps`**, whereas NTSC territories (North America, Japan, South Korea) broadcast at **`23.976` / `29.97` / `59.94 fps`** (or true integer **`24.00` / `30.00` / `60.00 fps`** for digital web/social delivery) ([SMPTE ST 12-1 Timecode](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fblog%2Funderstanding-standards-time-code)).
     Failure modes: Exporting H.264 with `Full Range (0–255)` while the player assumes `Legal/TV Range (16–235)` → all shadow detail below `IRE 6%` is crushed to solid black; omitting `+faststart` (`moov` atom at end) → web players stall for seconds before starting playback.
     Checklist: [ ] Mezzanine master archived in `10-bit` ProRes 422 HQ / `12-bit` 4444 or `16-bit` EXR [ ] Distribution `.mp4` uses Closed GOP (`≤ 1–2 s`), CABAC, progressive scan, and `+faststart` [ ] Bitrate scaled to resolution, frame rate, and grain/particle complexity [ ] Color metadata explicitly tagged (`NCLC 1-1-1`, `Legal/TV range 16–235 / 64–940`) [ ] Small typography carries luminance contrast ($\Delta Y \ge 0.35$) to survive `4:2:0` chroma subsampling
     Sources: [YouTube Recommended Upload Encoding Settings](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en); [ITU-R BT.709](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.709); [ITU-R BT.2100](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.itu.int%2Frec%2Fr-rec-bt.2100); [SMPTE Timecode Standards](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fblog%2Funderstanding-standards-time-code); [Netflix Delivery Specifications](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external).

### 20.2 Platform Delivery Specifications and SMPTE Title/Action Safe Margins

Definition: The platform-specific resolution, aspect ratio, frame-rate ceiling, bitrate re-compression behavior, and UI/overscan exclusion margins governing delivery to Netflix/Broadcast, YouTube, TikTok, and Instagram ([Netflix Partner Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external); [YouTube Help](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en); [TikTok Ads Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fads.tiktok.com%2Fresources%2Fhelp%2Farticle%2Ftiktok-auction-in-feed-ads%3Flang%3Den); [Meta Business Help](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.facebook.com%2Fbusiness%2Fhelp%2F1038071743007909); [SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den)).
Why:

- Emotion: Prevents awkward black pillar/letterbox bars or UI-obscured faces from breaking viewer immersion on the target device.
- Attention: Keeps focal action and CTAs inside the exact unobstructed viewport region of each platform.
- Production value: Uploading at `1080×1920` (`≤ 20 Mbps`) for TikTok/Reels avoids triggering aggressive platform server-side downscalers that crush `4K` uploads into blurry `720p` artifacts.
- Clarity: Ensures burned-in graphics, logos, and subtitles never collide with platform like/share icons or television bezel overscan.
- Cost: Following a unified Multi-Platform Safe-Margin Template allows automated multi-aspect crop/re-layout without manual rework.
  Rules:

1. Enforce the **Cross-Platform Delivery & Safe-Margin Specification Matrix**:

| Platform                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Primary Aspect Ratio & Native Resolution                                                                 | Frame Rate (`fps`)                                            | Recommended Upload Bitrate & Container                                                                    | Mandatory Safe Zone / Exclusion Margins                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------ | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Broadcast TV & Cinema Streaming ([SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den) / [Netflix](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external))** | `16:9` (`3840×2160` UHD or `1920×1080` HD) / `2.39:1` / `2.00:1`                                         | `23.976`, `24.00`, `25.00`, `29.97`, `50`, `59.94`            | `ProRes 422 HQ` / `IMF JPEG2000` (`10-bit+`), `-23 LUFS` (EBU) / `-24 LKFS` (ATSC) / `-27 LKFS` (Netflix) | **Action Safe**: `93% W × 93% H` (`3.5%` margin all sides).<br>**Title Safe**: `90% W × 90% H` (`5.0%` margin all sides, i.e., `96 px` left/right, `54 px` top/bottom @ `1080p`).                                                    |
| **YouTube Long-Form ([YouTube Spec](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en))**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `16:9` (`3840×2160` preferred to trigger `VP9`/`AV1` high-bitrate transcode, even for `1080p` masters)   | `24`, `25`, `30`, `48`, `50`, `60`                            | `.mp4` (`H.264`/`HEVC`), `40–68 Mbps` (`4K`) or `12–20 Mbps` (`1080p`), `-14 LUFS`                        | Keep `5%` perimeter clear; reserve bottom-right corner (`15% W × 12% H`) during final `10 seconds` for watermark/end-screen cards.                                                                                                   |
| **TikTok ([TikTok Ad Spec](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fads.tiktok.com%2Fresources%2Fhelp%2Farticle%2Ftiktok-auction-in-feed-ads%3Flang%3Den))**                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | **`9:16` (`1080×1920`)** (upload at exact `1080×1920`, _not_ `4K`, to avoid harsh server re-compression) | `30` or `60` (`30 fps` optimal for bitrate-per-frame quality) | `.mp4` / `.mov`, **`10–18 Mbps`** (`≤ 500 MB`), `-14 to -16 LUFS`                                         | Clear **Top `12%` (`230 px`)**, **Bottom `25%` (`480 px`)**, **Right `15%` (`160 px`)**, **Left `6%` (`65 px`)**. Active safe window: `X ∈ [65, 920]`, `Y ∈ [230, 1440]`.                                                            |
| **Instagram Reels / Stories ([Meta Spec](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.facebook.com%2Fbusiness%2Fhelp%2F1038071743007909))**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | **`9:16` (`1080×1920`)** (plus `4:5` `1080×1350` & `1:1` `1080×1080` profile grid crop check!)           | `30` (`24–60` supported)                                      | `.mp4`, **`10–16 Mbps`**, `AAC 48 kHz`, `-14 LUFS`                                                        | Clear **Top `11%` (`210 px`)**, **Bottom `22%` (`420 px`)**, **Right `13%` (`140 px`)**. Also keep thumbnail focal subject inside the center **`4:5` (`1080×1350`, `Y ∈ [285, 1635]`)** so profile grid previews aren't decapitated! |
| **YouTube Shorts**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **`9:16` (`1080×1920` or `2160×3840`)**                                                                  | `24`, `30`, `60`                                              | `.mp4`, `15–25 Mbps` (`1080p`), `-14 LUFS`                                                                | Clear **Top `10%` (`192 px`)**, **Bottom `22%` (`422 px`)**, **Right `14%` (`150 px`)**.                                                                                                                                             |

2. **The Universal Vertical Safe Box (`9:16` Cross-Posting Rule)**:
   - When authoring a single `9:16` (`1080×1920`) vertical master destined for simultaneous distribution across TikTok, Instagram Reels, and YouTube Shorts, constrain all essential typography, logos, and faces to the **Intersection Safe Box**:
     $$X \in [70\text{ px},\; 920\text{ px}] \quad (6.5\%\text{ to }85.2\%\text{ W}), \qquad Y \in [250\text{ px},\; 1420\text{ px}] \quad (13.0\%\text{ to }74.0\%\text{ H})$$
   - This single `850 × 1170 px` safe box simultaneously clears TikTok's right-hand engagement column, Reels' bottom caption drawer, Shorts' top status bar, AND stays 100% inside Instagram's `4:5` (`Y ∈ [285, 1635]`) profile grid crop!
     Event mapping: Viewport overlay validator `platform_safe_zone_validator(target_platforms=["smpte_2046", "tiktok", "reels", "shorts"], enforce_intersection_box=true, safe_x_norm=[0.065, 0.852], safe_y_norm=[0.130, 0.740])`.
     Raster/bake note: Render a toggleable semi-transparent diagnostic **Safe-Zone Guide Overlay** in preview builds showing SMPTE `93%/90%` boxes (for `16:9`) or the `850×1170` Universal Vertical Safe Box + `4:5` Grid Crop (for `9:16`), and strip the overlay automatically in the final production render pass.
     Cultural notes: While `16:9` television sets in modern homes rarely overscan by the `10%–20%` of 1970s CRT televisions, [SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den) `90%` Title Safe remains universally enforced by broadcast QC because resting typography `< 5%` from the physical monitor bezel creates uncomfortable visual edge tension.
     Failure modes: Uploading a `4K 60fps` `80 Mbps` file to TikTok or Instagram Reels → the platform's aggressive mobile ingestion transcoder crushes the file below `4 Mbps`, introducing worse macroblocking than uploading a pre-optimized `1080×1920 30fps` `14 Mbps` file; placing a hero title at `Y = 1650 px` on a `1080×1920` Reel → covered by the caption text and cropped out of the profile grid.
     Checklist: [ ] `16:9` masters keep action inside `93%` SMPTE ST 2046-1 Action Safe and titles inside `90%` Title Safe [ ] `9:16` vertical masters keep essential text/subjects inside `X ∈ [6.5%, 85.2%]`, `Y ∈ [13.0%, 74.0%]` [ ] `9:16` cover/thumbnail frame checked against center `4:5` (`1080×1350`) and `1:1` (`1080×1080`) crops [ ] TikTok/Reels delivered at native `1080×1920` (`10–18 Mbps`); YouTube delivered at `4K` or high-bitrate `1080p`
     Sources: [SMPTE ST 2046-1 Safe Areas Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den); [Netflix Partner Help Center](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external); [YouTube Recommended Upload Encoding Settings](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en); [TikTok In-Feed Ad Specifications](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fads.tiktok.com%2Fresources%2Fhelp%2Farticle%2Ftiktok-auction-in-feed-ads%3Flang%3Den); [Meta/Instagram Reels Specifications](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.facebook.com%2Fbusiness%2Fhelp%2F1038071743007909).

## 21. Review & Iteration

### 21.1 Quantitative Quality Rubrics, Versioning Conventions, Feedback Protocols, and Definition of Done

Definition: The deterministic inspection gates, semantic asset/render versioning schema, frame-accurate feedback syntax, and binary release checklist that govern collaborative iteration between planner/author users and artist/director users.
Why:

- Emotion: Objective numeric rubrics eliminate vague, frustrating creative friction ("make it pop" → "increase hero-to-background OKLab $\Delta L$ from `0.22` to `0.45`").
- Attention: Frame-indexed review protocols focus iteration strictly on the highest-leverage visual and auditory defects.
- Production value: Structured multi-stage review Gates (`Gate 1: Style Frame`, `Gate 2: Animatic`, `Gate 3: Rough Playblast`, `Gate 4: Final Graded/Mixed Master`) prevent shipping un-caught render glitches, dead holds, or sync offsets.
- Clarity: Translates subjective director intuition into unambiguous, machine-actionable event and shader deltas.
- Cost: Catching a timing error at `Gate 2 (Animatic)` or `Gate 3 (Viewport Playblast)` costs `10×–50×` less compute and authoring time than catching it after `Gate 4` lightmap/simulation bakes.
  Rules:

1. **Four-Gate Progressive Approval Pipeline**:
   - **Gate 1 — Brief, Color Script & Hero Style Frames (`Static`)**: Verify objective (`≤ 15 words`), OKLCH harmony ratios (`60:30:10` / `85:15`), BT.709 grayscale hierarchy ($\Delta L \ge 0.35$), and `10%` thumbnail silhouette read before any animation begins.
   - **Gate 2 — Frame-Locked Animatic / 3D Previs (`Stepped/Coarse Motion + VO/Beat Grid`)**: Verify total runtime, shot durations vs. complexity, WPM/CPS reading budgets, `180°/30°` camera continuity, and hit-point sync (`0f` to `-2f`). **Lock editorial cut timing at Gate 2** before starting heavy simulation bakes.
   - **Gate 3 — Motion & Baking Playblast (`Full Easing + Baked Caches, Flat/Viewport Shading`)**: Verify 12-principle mechanics (squash-and-stretch volume conservation, anticipation, `1–3f` channel overlap, `≤ 15f` ensemble stagger spread, moving holds, zero VAT/RBD interpenetration).
   - **Gate 4 — Final Composited, Graded & Mastered Deliverable (`Full Shader + Post + Mix`)**: Verify linear-light compositing, optical post-effect kitsch ceilings, WCAG contrast/PSE flash safety, LUFS/dBTP audio mastering, and platform safe zones.
2. **Semantic Versioning & Immutable Naming Convention**:
   - Name all scene descriptions, baked caches, and rendered outputs using deterministic semantic tokens:
     `{project_id}_{sequence_id}_{shot_id}_{stage}_v{MAJOR}.{MINOR}_{aspect}_{fps}fps.{ext}`
     (e.g., `projA_seq01_sh030_anim_v02.01_16x9_24fps.mp4`), where `MAJOR` increments on structural/timing changes and `MINOR` increments on shader/polish tweaks. Never overwrite a previous render; never use ambiguous suffixes like `_final_NEW_v2_real.mp4` ([OpenUSD Asset Resolution](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fopenusd.org%2Frelease%2Fintro.html)).
3. **Actionable Feedback Translation Protocol (No Vague Adjectives)**:
   - Every review note must specify: **(1) Exact SMPTE Timecode & Frame Range (`HH:MM:SS:FF` / `f_start–f_end`)**, **(2) Target Element ID**, **(3) Observable Defect Symptom**, and **(4) Quantitative Parameter Delta**:
     - _Forbidden_: `"The title entrance feels sluggish and hard to read."`
     - _Mandatory_: `"Frames 00:02:12–00:03:06 (f60–f78), element title_hero_01: linear ease feels mechanical and luminance contrast is 2.8:1. Change easing to expo_out (0.16, 1.0, 0.3, 1.0), reduce entrance duration from 28f to 14f, and darken background scrim by -0.8 EV to achieve contrast ≥ 7.0:1."`
4. **Quantitative 100-Point Production Rubric (Minimum Passing Score: `85/100`, Zero Critical Failures)**:
   - **Visual Hierarchy & Composition (`20 pts`)**: Focal $\Delta L \ge 0.35$, `≥ 35%` negative space, SMPTE/social safe zones respected, passes `10%` thumbnail check.
   - **Color & Pipeline Fidelity (`20 pts`)**: OKLCH harmony ratios (`60:30:10` / `85:15`), scene-linear compositing, zero 8-bit Mach banding (`TPDF dither active`), NCLC tags embedded.
   - **Kinetic & Animation Craft (`20 pts`)**: Appropriate easing families, anticipation/follow-through present, `1–3f` channel overlap, `≤ 15f` stagger spread, `180°` shutter motion blur.
   - **Editorial & Attention Flow (`20 pts`)**: `Frame 0` active launch, cuts on action (`-2f`) or J/L splits, eye-trace continuity (`≤ 15% W`), reading hold formula satisfied.
   - **Audio, Accessibility & Delivery (`20 pts`)**: Hits on `-1f` to `0f` transient, VO intelligibility (`+8–14 LU` over ducked music), exact target LUFS/dBTP, WCAG contrast `≥ 4.5:1` and PSE flash limit `≤ 3 Hz` passed (_any PSE violation or missing license = automatic `0/100` critical failure_).
     Event mapping: Review manifest `review_feedback_log(version="v02.01", gate=4, rubric_score=92, notes=[{frame_range: [60, 78], target_id: "title_hero_01", channel: "easing", from: "linear", to: "expo_out"}])`.
     Raster/bake note: Render a burned-in **Review Slate & HUD** on `Gate 2` and `Gate 3` review exports (displaying `SMPTE Timecode`, `Global Frame Number`, `Shot ID`, `Version String`, `Camera Focal Length`, and `Active LUT/Color Space` in the outer `3.5%` margin outside Action Safe) so feedback always cites exact frame numbers; disable the HUD on `Gate 4` final delivery.
     Cultural notes: High-volume studio pipelines (studio dailies and synchronous frame-review sessions) enforce frame-numbered burn-ins and non-judgmental, parameter-specific notes focused on how the frame directs the audience's eye `[OPINION]`.
     Failure modes: Rendering `4K` final shaders and baked simulations before locking the edit timing in an animatic → `70%` of rendered frames are thrown away when the voiceover is trimmed; giving feedback in un-quantified adjectives ("make it edgier") → endless guessing loops.
     Checklist: [ ] Passed sequentially through Gate 1 (Style Frame), Gate 2 (Animatic), Gate 3 (Playblast), and Gate 4 (Final Master) [ ] Files versioned semantically (`_vMAJOR.MINOR_`) with burned-in frame/timecode HUD on review builds [ ] All feedback notes converted to `(Frame Range, Element ID, Symptom, Numeric Delta)` [ ] Scores `≥ 85/100` on the Quantitative Production Rubric with zero critical safety/legal failures
     Sources: [OpenUSD Documentation](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fopenusd.org%2Frelease%2Fintro.html); [SMPTE Timecode Standards](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fblog%2Funderstanding-standards-time-code); [Netflix VFX \& Editorial Review Specifications](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fbranded-vfx-media-review-delivery-specifications).

## 22. Legal & Rights Clearance

### 22.1 Licensing Verification: Music, Fonts, Stock Footage, 3D Assets, and Likeness Rights

Definition: The chain-of-title verification and license-scope audit governing all third-party intellectual property (music composition/master rights, typeface broadcast/embedding EULAs, stock video/textures, 3D meshes, trademarks, and human vocal/visual likenesses) incorporated into a production.
Why:

- Emotion: N/A — Legal and rights clearance operates as a binary institutional compliance layer rather than an affective viewer cue.
- Attention: N/A — Rights metadata is invisible on screen (except mandatory end-credit attributions when required by license).
- Production value: Prevents visible watermarks, unlicensed font substitutions, or sudden post-release copyright takedowns/mutes that destroy a campaign's public launch.
- Clarity: Provides an auditable manifest of territory, duration, media channel (organic web vs. paid broadcast/ad spend), and seat/impression caps for every external asset.
- Cost: Auditing license scope at `Gate 1 (Pre-Production)` prevents catastrophic mid-campaign legal injunctions, Content-ID Demonetization/Muting, or emergency re-scoring and re-rendering.
  Rules:

1. **Music Synchronization + Master Recording Clearance**:
   - Every musical track requires clearance of **two distinct copyrights**: (a) the **Musical Composition (Publishing / Sync License)** from the songwriter/publisher, and (b) the **Master Sound Recording (Master Use License)** from the label/recording artist (or a unified Royalty-Free / Direct-License agreement covering both).
   - Verify that the license explicitly covers the **Distribution Tier**: _Standard Creator / Organic Social_ licenses routinely forbid **Paid Advertising Spend (Boosted Posts / Pre-Roll / Broadcast TV / OTT)** or theatrical distribution above specific impression caps. Log the **Cue Sheet** (Track Title, Composer, Publisher, PRO affiliation `ASCAP/BMI/PRS`, and exact duration `MM:SS`) and clear platform **Content-ID Allowlisting** before launch `[OPINION]`.
2. **Font & Typeface EULA Verification (Desktop vs. Broadcast vs. App Embedding)**:
   - Audit every typeface file (`.otf`, `.ttf`, `.woff2`) against its specific usage model:
     - **Rendered Raster Video (`pixels baked into .mp4`)**: Most standard commercial Desktop EULAs permit rendering static/animated outlines into rasterized video frames, **EXCEPT** many foundry EULAs require a separate **Broadcast / Commercial Advertising Rider** when used in television commercials, paid video ads, or theatrical titles, or forbid using a font as a primary registered **Wordmark Logo** without a Logo License.
     - **Declarative / Interactive Vector Delivery (`.json` Lottie, WebGL, HTML, App UI)**: Embedding the actual `.ttf`/`.otf`/`.woff2` font binary inside a distributable package requires an explicit **App / Web / Digital Ads Embedding License** (or converting short static display titles to raw Bézier spline outlines so no font binary is distributed) ([Lottie v1.0 Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Flottie.github.io%2Flottie-spec%2F1.0%2F)).
     - **Open-Source Safe Fallback**: Open Font License (**SIL OFL 1.1**) typefaces permit unrestricted commercial raster broadcast, modification, and binary embedding without impression caps.
3. **3D Models, Textures, HDRIs, and Stock Footage**:
   - Verify that no 3D mesh, PBR texture scan, or stock plate carries an **"Editorial Use Only"** restriction (which strictly prohibits commercial promotion or advertising).
   - Never redistribute raw, un-encrypted third-party 3D meshes or textures inside an open, downloadable scene archive if the asset license permits _rendered output only_ (bake or lock assets per vendor EULA).
   - Audit background 3D/2D environments for **Incidental Trademarks, Trade Dress, and Copyrighted Art** (e.g., recognizable third-party brand logos on props, copyrighted architectural landmarks requiring commercial property releases, or recognizable vehicle trade dress): remove or replace all third-party logos with neutral geometric designs before baking textures.
4. **Human Likeness, Voice, and Synthetic/AI Provenance**:
   - Secure signed **Talent Model & Vocal Releases** for every recognizable human face, silhouette, or voice recording, explicitly specifying territory (`Worldwide`), term (`Perpetual` or `N years`), and media (`Organic + Paid Digital/Broadcast`).
   - Never clone, synthesize, or imitate a real person's voice or facial likeness without explicit written contractual consent; maintain a complete **Asset Provenance Manifest** logging the origin and license URI of every asset in the workspace.
     Event mapping: Scene root compliance manifest `legal_provenance_manifest(assets=[{asset_id, type: "music"|"font"|"mesh"|"voice", license_tier: "commercial_paid_ads"|"sil_ofl"|"cc0", territory: "worldwide", expiration_date: "perpetual", cue_sheet_logged: true}])`.
     Raster/bake note: When delivering declarative vector scenes (such as [Lottie JSON](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Flottie.github.io%2Flottie-spec%2F1.0%2F)) where font binary embedding rights are unavailable, convert text layers to baked Bézier path geometry (`chars_to_splines = true`) during export compilation.
     Cultural notes: Intellectual property rules on architectural landmarks vary by jurisdiction: for example, while daytime views of the Eiffel Tower are public domain, its nighttime illuminated lighting design is protected under French copyright law and requires clearance for commercial video; European GDPR and US state right-of-publicity statutes strictly govern biometric voice and facial likeness.
     Failure modes: Using an "Organic Social Only" music track or "Free for Personal Use" font in a paid commercial video campaign → automatic platform audio muting, DMCA takedown, and retroactive statutory copyright damages; leaving a recognizable third-party brand logo on a downloaded 3D prop mesh → client legal rejection at final delivery.
     Checklist: [ ] Music cleared for both Sync (Composition) and Master rights matching Paid/Broadcast/Organic tier [ ] Music Cue Sheet and Content-ID allowlist logged [ ] Font EULAs verified for Broadcast/Commercial video and/or binary vector embedding (or SIL OFL 1.1 used) [ ] All 3D meshes, textures, and stock plates cleared for Commercial Use (zero "Editorial Only" assets or incidental third-party trademarks) [ ] Signed Talent/Voice Releases and complete Asset Provenance Manifest archived
     Sources: [Lottie v1.0 Specification](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Flottie.github.io%2Flottie-spec%2F1.0%2F); [Netflix Delivery Specifications \& Requirements](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fstudiopartner.netflix.net%2Fstudio%2Fdelivery-specifications-and-requirements-external).

## Cross-Scope Evaluation Matrix Summary (§3 Scope × §4 Axes)

The table below synthesizes how all 22 scope domains map across the 6 mandatory evaluation axes defined in §4:

| Scope Section                    | Cultural & Meta-Cultural Axis                                                                                   | Emotion Axis (Mechanism)                                                                      | Attention Axis (Gaze & Salience)                                                                | Production Value Axis ("Cheap Tells" Avoided)                                                                  | Clarity Axis (Comprehension)                                                                            | Cost Axis (Render / Bake / Authoring Trade-Off)                                                                            |
| :------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **1. Pre-production**            | Regional high-context mood vs. direct-response hook norms; Swiss/Bauhaus/anime style anchors.                   | Locks valence/arousal trajectory and vocal breathing room before scene build.                 | Budgets WPM (`130–150`) and CPS (`≤ 15`) to prevent dual-coding overload.                       | Eliminates mid-project style drift and rushed voiceover pacing.                                                | Enforces a single `≤ 15-word` proposition and grayscale thumbnail check.                                | Static style frames + animatic catch `90%` of flaws at `< 5%` of full render cost.                                         |
| **2. 2D Composition**            | Swiss modular grids (`12×8`) vs. East Asian _ma_ negative space vs. variety _telop_ density.                    | Symmetry = monumental authority; asymmetry + negative space (`≥ 35%`) = tension/poise.        | Places anchors at `1/3` or `1/φ` nodes; keeps gaze jump `≤ 15% W` across cuts.                  | Eliminates `2–8 px` near-miss alignments, frame-edge tangents, and UI-clipped text.                            | 3-level scale hierarchy ($\ge 1.414:1$) and macro/micro whitespace ($\ge 2.5:1$).                       | Parametric grid coordinates adapt automatically across aspect ratios.                                                      |
| **3. Typography**                | Neo-Grotesque objectivity vs. Didone fashion vs. CJK weight matching.                                           | Type morphology sets instant tone; kinetic prosody mirrors vocal emotion.                     | Masked baseline reveals + word/line staggers (`2–6f`) pace eye movement.                        | Eliminates proportional-numeral counter jitter (`tnum`), hairline shimmer, and `0 em` display tracking.        | Enforces cap height `≥ 2.2% H`, inverse tracking, and stationary reading hold formula.                  | MSDF fragment shaders keep glyphs sharp at any zoom at minimal VRAM cost.                                                  |
| **4. Color**                     | Cross-cultural color semiotics (White/Red/Gold); Bauhaus triads vs. cinema Teal-Orange.                         | OKLab Lightness ($L$) and Chroma ($C$) drive arousal/valence; warm/cool drives depth.         | Reserves `10%–15%` high-chroma complement strictly for the primary focal anchor.                | Eliminates sRGB gradient dead zones, `50:50` vibrating complements, and 8-bit Mach banding.                    | Enforces $\Delta L \ge 0.35$ value contrast and constant-$L$ categorical palettes.                      | Analytic OKLab shader math + `±1 LSB` blue-noise dithering costs `< 0.1 ms/frame`.                                         |
| **5. Color Pipeline**            | Cinema ACES (`ACEScg`) vs. stylized `AgX`; broadcast `BT.709` (`2.4`) vs. web `sRGB` (`2.2`) vs. HDR `BT.2100`. | Filmic sigmoid highlight roll-off preserves organic photochemical warmth.                     | Keeps specular highlights and emissive accents luminous without harsh channel clipping.         | Eliminates `Raw` normal-map gamma bugs, dark alpha fringes, and QuickTime `1-1-1` gamma shifts.                | Normalizes shot-to-shot exposure and preserves brand hex accuracy via post-ODT/inverse-ODT rules.       | Single baked `65³` tetrahedral 3D LUT + log shaper evaluates in 1 GPU instruction.                                         |
| **6. 2D Motion Graphics**        | Flat corporate vector vs. textured tactile luma mattes; Kurosawa wipes vs. Edgar Wright whips.                  | Rounded caps/curves = warmth; sharp miters = precision; exponential zooms = propulsion.       | Arc-length trim tracers and velocity-matched transitions carry gaze across cuts.                | Eliminates non-uniform stroke distortion, inside-out morph twists, and linear-scale zoom kinks.                | Connects complex diagram nodes via trim paths and object mask reveals.                                  | Pre-normalized spline vertex counts and stencil mattes render in microseconds.                                             |
| **7. Animation Fundamentals**    | Western fluid `1s` (`24 fps`) arcs/overlap vs. Japanese limited `2s/3s` holds + smear frames.                   | Volume-preserving squash/stretch, anticipation (`4–10f`), and damped springs impart life.     | High initial velocity (`expo-out`) captures peripheral vision on Frame 1.                       | Eliminates robotic linear/symmetrical `ease-in-out`, "twinning", and frozen dead holds.                        | Long deceleration tails keep elements readable during the final `40%` of transit.                       | Closed-form spring/Bézier shaders and `≤ 15f` stagger functions replace hand keyframing.                                   |
| **8. 3D Composition**            | Deep-focus cinema (_Welles_) vs. macro shallow-DoF product films vs. miniature clay dioramas.                   | Key-to-Fill ratio (`2:1` high-key vs. `8:1+` low-key) and depth planes sculpt mood.           | Sharp midground + DoF blur + atmospheric haze isolate the hero Z-plane.                         | Eliminates flat frontal flash lighting, double fill shadows, and "floating object" missing AO.                 | Rim lighting (`135°–165°`) separates dark silhouettes from dark backgrounds.                            | Baked directional `UV1` lightmaps (`16–32 texels/m`) + L2 SH probes replace runtime GI.                                    |
| **9. Camera & Cinematography**   | Ozu low `50mm` stillness vs. `85–135mm` hardware beauty shots vs. documentary handheld.                         | Low/High angles set power dynamics; Dolly Push-In builds intimacy; Zolly induces shock.       | Lateral `Truck`/`Orbit` activates parallax; `30°` and `180°` rules protect orientation.         | Eliminates wide-angle close-up distortion, `6-DOF` spline wobble, and `10°–35°/s` 24p pan judder.              | Matches shot size (`EWS`→`ECU`) and shutter angle (`180°` vs. `90°`) to action density.                 | 2-Node camera rig + `16–24 tap` screen-space velocity blur deliver physical optics in `< 0.5 ms`.                          |
| **10. Materials & Shading**      | Tactile industrial minimalism (matte vs. polished bevel) vs. anime Cel/TAM halftone shaders.                    | Surface micro-roughness and chromatic cel shadows trigger tactile empathy.                    | Bevel specular highlights (`1–3 segments`) and light-modulated outlines trace form edges.       | Eliminates `[0,0,0]`/`[1,1,1]` non-physical albedos, `0 mm` sharp CG edges, and specular aliasing fireflies.   | Clamps high-frequency patterns above the `2.5 px` Nyquist limit to prevent moiré.                       | Split-sum GGX IBL, Screen-Space Refraction/SSS, and MatCaps avoid ray tracing.                                             |
| **11. 3D Motion Design**         | Satisfying kinetic cloner loops vs. Western multi-viseme lip-sync vs. anime `3-state` mouth flaps.              | Eye-lead saccades (`2–3f` before head) and contrapposto weight shifts convey thought.         | Spatial falloff effectors sweep waves of transformation across `10⁴` instances.                 | Eliminates Frenet-frame $180^\circ$ curve twists, un-remeshed glyph cracks, and late lip-sync.                 | Viseme peaks lead audio by `1–2f` (`M/B/P` locked `≥ 2f`) for effortless speech reading.                | GPU compute instancers and Dual-Quaternion Skinning run in `< 1 ms`.                                                       |
| **12. Simulation & Procedural**  | Luxury slow-motion cloth/liquid macro loops (`0.3g`) vs. stylized stepped-timing VFX shards.                    | Viscous fluids, billowing silk, and 3-tier RBD destruction trigger somatic impact.            | Curl-noise particle streams and fracture bursts direct gaze along force vectors.                | Eliminates uniform particle sizes, pre-cracked RBD seams, and hard billboard floor intersections.              | Poisson-disk ($d \ge 2r$) and Golden-Angle ($137.5^\circ$) layouts prevent messy asset overlaps.        | Offline baking to `fp16` VATs, transform caches, and 6-light optical-flow flipbooks costs `< 2 ms` at runtime.             |
| **13. Compositing**              | ACEScg multi-pass EXR VFX pipelines vs. nostalgic 16mm halation/grain vs. clean Swiss tech UI.                  | Linear mip-bloom (`3%–8%`), halation, and $\cos^4\theta$ vignette add optical warmth.         | Cryptomatte isolation and light wraps (`8%–20%`) lock foreground subjects into the plate.       | Eliminates double-premultiplied dark halos, filtered Z-depth fringes, and full-screen CA/glow kitsch.          | Unpremultiplied grading preserves crisp anti-aliased text and edge colors.                              | Single-pass MRT AOV output + Karis downsample bloom pyramid executes in `< 0.8 ms`.                                        |
| **14. Editing**                  | Soviet collision montage vs. Hollywood invisible continuity (`Murch Rule of Six`).                              | Prioritizes Emotion (`51%`) and Story (`23%`); `L-Cuts` (`12–36f`) let reactions land.        | `Cut on Action` (`-2f` to `-3f`) and `Match Cuts` (`≤ 3% W` offset) hide the edit seam.         | Eliminates monotonous butt-cuts and metronomic zero-variance shot lengths ($\text{CV} \ge 0.25$).              | Scales shot duration to visual complexity (`CU`: `0.5–2s`; `EWS`: `3–6s`).                              | Timeline trim adjustments in the sequence manifest cost zero render compute.                                               |
| **15. Attention & Short-Form**   | Global vertical feed scroll norms (`TikTok`/`Reels`/`Shorts`) vs. tactile ASMR "anti-chaos" hooks.              | Open-loop hooks (`0–3s`) spark curiosity; seamless loop endings (`end → 0`) delight.          | Frame `0` active velocity + `3–5s` pattern interrupts + central captions hold gaze.             | Eliminates black cold-opens, decapitated `16:9` center-crops, and unreadable muted videos.                     | Passes the `100%` Muted Sound-Off Comprehension Audit.                                                  | Modular `+Y` vertical stacking adapts `16:9` scenes to `9:16` without rebuilding assets.                                   |
| **16. Music & Sound Design**     | Trailer 3-act silence drops vs. dense short-form beat cuts vs. pitched anime/arcade FM UI sounds.               | Audio-visual _synchresis_ (`0f` to `-2f` lead) and `0.5–1.5s` strategic silence build chills. | Transient hits + stereo-panned whooshes (`±45%`) orient spatial attention.                      | Eliminates late "mushy" visual hits, every-beat cutting fatigue, and dead digital room-tone dropouts.          | Tactile Foley and key-quantized UI chimes clarify material and data states (`≤ 3` simultaneous sounds). | Integer-frame BPMs (`120 BPM` = `12f` @ `24fps`) and event-bound SFX triggers automate sync.                               |
| **17. Mix & Mastering**          | Wide-dynamic cinema (`10–18 LU` LRA) vs. compressed mobile mixes (`4–8 LU` LRA); EBU R128 vs. ATSC A/85.        | Presence-boosted (`2.5–4.5 kHz`), tightly leveled VO conveys calm authority.                  | Spectral sidechain ducking (`-8 to -14 LU` margin) prevents listener fatigue.                   | Eliminates word-to-word ducking pump (`400 ms` hold), AAC transcode clipping (`-1.5 dBTP`), and quiet uploads. | Guarantees `100%` consonant intelligibility even on mono smartphone speakers (`300 Hz–10 kHz`).         | Standardized bus DSP + synchronous `48 kHz / 24-bit` stem bounce (`Full`, `M&E`, `DX`, `MX`, `SFX`) prevents QC rejection. |
| **18. Narrative & Structure**    | Western Three-Act / `PAS` conflict arcs vs. East Asian 4-act _Kishōtenketsu_ perspective twists.                | Binds visual contrast directly to Story Intensity peaking at `75%–88%` runtime.               | Visual reveal leads verbal label by `6–12f`; Given-Before-New bridges shots.                    | Eliminates flat non-escalating intensity, cognitive overload (`> 3` chunks), and multi-CTA confusion.          | Enforces `1` proposition per shot and `1` isolated CTA (`≤ 6 words`, held `≥ 2.5–4.0s`).                | Concentrates `50%+` of simulation/lighting compute into the Hook (`0–10%`) and Climax (`75–88%`).                          |
| **19. Accessibility**            | Global regulatory mandates (WCAG 2.2, Ofcom Harding, Japan NAB PSE rules, Netflix Timed Text).                  | Protects viewers from vestibular nausea and photosensitive seizures.                          | High luminance contrast (`≥ 7.0:1` over video) and cut-snapped captions aid effortless reading. | Eliminates un-backed white text over bright footage, cut-straddling subtitles, and `> 3 Hz` strobes.           | Redundant non-hue coding (lightness + shape/pattern) ensures full CVD comprehension.                    | Automated compute-shader contrast & PSE rolling-window validators catch defects pre-render.                                |
| **20. Delivery**                 | PAL (`25/50 fps`) vs. NTSC (`23.976/29.97/60 fps`); Broadcast SMPTE `2046-1` vs. Vertical Social UI zones.      | `10-bit` HEVC/ProRes + legal range signaling preserve intended shadow/highlight mood.         | Universal Vertical Safe Box (`850×1170 px`) keeps subjects clear of all platform UI overlays.   | Eliminates `4:2:0` red/blue text smear, `Full vs. Legal` crushed blacks, and `4K` social re-compression blur.  | Embeds explicit `NCLC 1-1-1` (SDR) or `9-16-9` (HDR PQ) metadata and `+faststart`.                      | Rendering once to a `10/12-bit` ProRes/EXR Mezzanine Master feeds all distribution transcodes.                             |
| **21. Review & Iteration**       | Studio _dailies_ / frame-burn-in culture vs. parameter-driven declarative authoring.                            | Replaces subjective friction with calm, objective numeric alignment.                          | Focuses iteration on frame-accurate visual/audio salience defects.                              | Eliminates shipping un-caught render glitches via the `85/100` Quantitative Rubric.                            | Converts vague adjectives into `(Frame Range, Element ID, Symptom, Numeric Delta)`.                     | Four-Gate pipeline (Style Frame → Animatic → Playblast → Final) prevents costly late re-bakes.                             |
| **22. Legal & Rights Clearance** | Jurisdictional landmark copyright (e.g., nighttime Eiffel Tower) and biometric likeness laws.                   | N/A — Compliance layer; operates invisibly outside affective viewer experience.               | N/A — Compliance metadata does not direct on-screen viewer gaze.                                | Prevents watermarks, missing-font fallbacks, and public DMCA/Content-ID muting.                                | Clear Asset Provenance Manifest documents territory, term, and media tier for every asset.              | Pre-clearing Sync+Master music, SIL OFL/Broadcast fonts, and Commercial 3D assets prevents legal takedowns.                |

## 23. Glossary of Production & Pipeline Terms

| Canonical Term                                       | Definition in This Manual                                                                                                                                                      | Cross-Industry Synonyms (Film / 3D / Motion / Game / Audio)                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| :--------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **180-Degree Rule**                                  | Spatial continuity constraint keeping all cameras on one side of the primary axis of action between subjects.                                                                  | Line of Action, Axis of Action, Director's Line, Screen Direction Grammar ([Mascelli](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.silmanjamespress.com%2Fshop%2Ffilmmaking-directing%2Fthe-five-cs-of-cinematography%2F))                                |
| **30-Degree Rule**                                   | Rule requiring consecutive shots of the same subject to differ by $\ge 30^\circ$ camera angle and/or $\ge 35\%$ scale.                                                         | Angle-Plus-Distance Rule, Anti-Jump-Cut Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **ACES / ACEScg**                                    | Academy Color Encoding System; `ACEScg` is the scene-linear AP1-primaries working space for CG rendering and compositing.                                                      | Scene-Linear Wide Gamut, AP1 Linear, OCIO Working Space ([ACES](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fdocs.acescentral.com%2F))                                                                                                                        |
| **Action Safe / Title Safe**                         | Protective interior raster margins (`93%` Action Safe, `90%` Title Safe per SMPTE ST 2046-1) plus platform UI exclusion zones.                                                 | Safe Area, Overscan Margin, UI Safe Zone, Essential Graphics Area ([SMPTE ST 2046-1](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.smpte.org%2Fhubfs%2FSMPTE%2520Standards%2520Quarterly%2520Report%2520September%25202024%2520%281%29.pdf%3FhsLang%3Den)) |
| **Albedo (Base Color)**                              | Intrinsic diffuse reflectance ratio of a dielectric surface (`[0.03, 0.85]` linear) or $F_0$ specular tint of a conductor.                                                     | Diffuse Color, Base Color, Surface Reflectance ([Burley](https://www.google.com/search?q=https%3A%2F%2Fdisneyanimation.com%2Fpublications%2Fphysically-based-shading-at-disney%2F))                                                                            |
| **Ambient Occlusion (AO)**                           | Ratio of non-occluded hemisphere above a surface point, applied strictly to indirect diffuse/specular light.                                                                   | Crevice Shadow, Cavity Map, GTAO, HBAO+, Dirt Map                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **Animatic**                                         | Frame-accurate sequence of storyboard panels or coarse 3D playblasts timed to scratch voiceover and music beat grid.                                                           | Leica Reel, Story Reel, Previs Cut, Timed Board                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **Anticipation**                                     | Pre-action counter-movement (`8%–18%` opposite travel over `4–10 frames`) preparing the viewer's eye for a primary launch.                                                     | Wind-Up, Pre-Action Cue, Take                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **AOV (Arbitrary Output Variable)**                  | Isolated shading component buffer (Diffuse, Specular, Emission, Z-Depth, Velocity, Cryptomatte) output by the renderer.                                                        | Render Pass, Render Element, MRT Attachment, G-Buffer Channel                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **Associated Alpha (Premultiplied)**                 | Alpha representation where RGB channels are pre-scaled by coverage $\alpha$, enabling unified Porter-Duff `Over` compositing of opaque, translucent, and additive glow pixels. | Premultiplied Alpha (vs. Unassociated / Straight Alpha)                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Average Shot Length (ASL)**                        | Mean duration of shots within a sequence or act ($\mu_{\text{ASL}}$), paired with Coefficient of Variation ($\sigma / \mu$).                                                   | Cut Rate, Editorial Pace, Shot Rhythm                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **Bake**                                             | Offline precomputation of expensive simulations, global illumination, or procedural deformations into static textures or caches.                                               | Cache, Precompute, Lightmap Bake, Geometry Freeze, Point Cache                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
